Benedetta sosta. La pausa per le nazionali arriva nel momento giusto per l'Inter. Infatti i nerazzurri nel giro di due giorni hanno perso tre giocatori: Bonny, Thuram e Stankovic. È soprattutto in attacco l'emergenza maggiore per Cristian Chivu che nelle ultime settimane ha dovuto fare a meno anche di Lautaro e che spera di ritrovare al rientro dalla sosta.

"Nel frattempo Lautaro Martinez lavorerà per tornare tra i convocati di Chivu alla ripresa del campionato. Il capitano dell’Inter dovrebbe quindi sedere in panchina contro l’Atalanta dopo la sosta per le nazionali. Ma ovviamente, ancora di più nel contesto attuale, non verrà rischiato alcunché", si legge su Tuttosport.

"Ovviamente Chivu, oltre ad augurarsi che Pio Esposito torni dagli impegni con l’Italia esattamente come è partito, ossia al 100% e in forma, pensa chiaramente alle possibili soluzioni per ovviare ai problemi nel reparto d’attacco. Diouf potrebbe così essere così spostato dalla fascia e schierato a supporto dell’eventuale unica punta. Discorso che potenzialmente vale anche per Mkhitaryan, Sucic e Jones. Ma attenzione anche al possibile cambio modulo, con un 3-4-2-1 che porterebbe i due centrocampisti avanzati a svolgere compiti differenti e più offensivi, rispetto all’impostazione classica in mediana del 3-5-2. Modulo che resterebbe tale e non verrebbe toccato di una virgola se il tecnico romeno puntasse sui giovani Lavelli e Mosconi là davanti".

ImagePullBackOff on an image nobody had touched since March

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 11 • Originally published at benchlet.com

Our WebRTC signaling server was unavailable for 2 days, 17 hours and 55 minutes, from 2026-11-08 13:46 to 2026-11-11 07:41 UTC. The last commit to its code was on 2026-03-23 and the Deployment had pointed at the same image tag since then. The outage started with a disk filling up on the node. It lasted because of what a registry cron job had done to that image on 2026-03-24.

This is a two-node k3s cluster that I run alone for a few side projects. Times are UTC.

By the morning of 2026-11-11 the Deployment's ReplicaSet had three pods. The original, created in March, had ended on 2026-11-08 at 13:46:42 with exit code 0 and phase Succeeded. A replacement created one second later had never run and ended with ContainerStatusUnknown, exit code 137. A second replacement, created at 16:10:52, was Pending with this event repeating every five minutes:

The audit tool I mention below counted 1,565 of these events in 24 hours on that node. On the registry side, the server log had 1,068 requests for that manifest digest since 2026-11-09 23:58, every one answered 404 manifest unknown.

The usual causes of manifest unknown did not fit. The tag existed in the registry's filesystem. The same node had pulled other images from the same registry that afternoon. The Deployment had not changed since March.

I found the outage on 2026-11-11 at 05:08, from a read-only audit of the cluster (kubegap) that I had run for another reason. It listed the Deployment with zero available replicas and the pull failures next to it. The investigation then went through the disk, the registry, and the node cache, in that order.

The node's root filesystem is 106.7 GiB. On 2026-11-08 free space went from 19.4 GiB at 12:45 to 2.6 GiB at 13:48. The node reported DiskPressure from 13:47 to 14:10 and again from 16:10 to 16:20.

I caused this. That afternoon I pushed 25 deploy commits for a new service, and one of them shipped a 4.5 GiB image with the service's ML models inside. The Deployment picked it up at 13:37 and the pod started at 13:40. The kubelet's evictionHard is imagefs.available<5% and nodefs.available<5%; on k3s with a single root filesystem those are the same disk. When a node crosses that threshold the kubelet first reclaims node-level resources, which for an image filesystem means deleting every image no container is using, and then evicts pods if it is still under. The jump from 2.6 GiB to 27.3 GiB free between 13:48 and 14:05 is consistent with that. By 16:15 the node's eviction counters read 23 for imagefs and 27 for nodefs, and 16 pods across the cluster carried the Evicted reason.

The signaling pod is not among those 16. It ended at 13:46:42, one minute before the DiskPressure condition was recorded, with exit code 0. That is not what an eviction looks like, and I could not read the kubelet journal on that node that day, so I do not know what stopped it. The sequence I can show is: the container stopped inside the disk-pressure window; the first replacement died during the second window; the second replacement needed an image that was no longer in the node's store.

The models-in-image problem got the obvious fix the same evening: models on a volume, 1.6 GiB image.

The pull was failing on a tag that had been pulled without trouble in March. The registry is docker/distribution 2.8.3 with filesystem storage, so I looked at the files:

The tag pointed at an image index. The index was there. Both manifests the index pointed at had been unlinked from the repository, and 6 of the 10 blobs behind the platform manifest were gone. The revisions directory had last been written at 04:00:01 on 2026-03-24, the night after the push.

04:00 is when the registry's garbage-collection CronJob runs. From mid-March until 2026-06-02 that job ran registry garbage-collect --delete-untagged=true. I had turned the flag on to reclaim disk. I turned it off after another image broke, and did not go back to check what else the flag had removed.

This was reported as distribution/distribution#3004 in 2019 and again as #3178 in 2020. The fix, #4285, was merged in April 2024 and is only in the 3.x releases. 2.8.3, the last 2.x release, predates it.

containerd on the node had the image, and with the default imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent for a non-latest tag nothing asks the registry for an image that is already present. The pod restarted five times between March and November, most recently on 2026-10-28, each time from the local store. The first client to ask the registry for the platform manifest after March was the replacement pod on 2026-11-08, after the cached copy had been removed.

Knowing what to look for, I checked every image tag referenced by a running workload against the registry filesystem:

All six had been pushed in March 2026 while the flag was on: four on the 14th between 19:02 and 19:14, two on the 23rd. Every image pushed after May was either a single manifest or an intact index. Five of the six broken ones were still running from the node's cache, with no pullable copy anywhere.

Rebuild from the same commit with two lines added to the workflow:

With no attestation and one platform, buildx pushes a single application/vnd.oci.image.manifest.v1+json, and there is no index child for this collector to orphan. The new tag resolved to that manifest with every referenced blob present. The pod was ready at 07:41:37 on 2026-11-11.

Then the other five. Four of those repositories had CI that pushed to GitHub's container registry and edited manifest paths that no longer existed, so whatever had put their images into our registry in March, it was not the workflow in the repo. Three of the four built successfully and failed at the deploy step before I noticed. I pointed the four workflows at the real registry and the real manifest paths, rebuilt, and by 09:20 all six workloads were running from complete single-manifest images.

The alert rule existed and fired. KubeDeploymentReplicasMismatch went pending at 14:00 and firing at 14:30 on 2026-11-08 and stayed firing until the fix. Alertmanager's route sends everything above info to a Slack channel and repeats every four hours. I have not gone back through the channel history, and I did not act on it. One week earlier, on 2026-11-01, that channel already had nine alerts firing at the same time, including a TURN server that the audit dates as crash-looping since September. The rule was there. The channel was not being read.

If you run, or have ever run, docker/distribution 2.x with --delete-untagged, check every multi-manifest image pushed in that window by asking for the platform manifest, not the tag. A running pod only proves that the node has the image.

Public post-incident reports where pods could not come back because the registry could not serve their image are collected under the image pull failures symptom in Incident Library.

Originally published as a kubegap guide on benchlet.com.

Stop Cursor and Claude Code From Inventing Colors (A Token + Rules Setup That Sticks)

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 11 • Originally published at constrainly.com

If you build UI with Cursor or Claude Code, you've probably had this happen. You ask for a settings page, it looks fine at first glance, and then you start noticing things. The save button is bg-blue-500, but your brand color is teal. One card has 18px of padding while every other card has 16. A grey shows up that isn't in any file you wrote.

None of this is the model being sloppy. It has no idea what your design rules are, so it falls back on whatever is most common in the code it learned from. You can correct it in chat, but that correction only lives as long as the conversation does.

What actually fixes it is boring: put your design values in code, give the agent a short rules file it reads every time, and check the output with two searches. Here's the whole setup.

The rules file needs something to point at, so tokens come first. Plain CSS works fine:

Two small things here save a lot of cleanup later. Hover and focus get their own tokens, because if they don't, the agent invents a "slightly darker" shade every time it needs one. And dark mode changes the values behind the names, not the names themselves, so components never need a dark-mode color of their own.

If you use Tailwind, adding your colors isn't enough. The default palette is still sitting there, and blue-500 is a class the model has seen more often than any of your tokens. Clear the defaults first:

After this, bg-blue-500 just doesn't exist in your project. If the agent writes it, the element comes out unstyled and you spot it right away. On Tailwind v3 the equivalent is putting colors under theme.colors instead of theme.extend.colors, since extend keeps the full default palette around.

Spacing you can leave alone. Tailwind's spacing already runs on a 4px step, so you only need to say which steps are allowed. If you haven't settled on a scale, I wrote up a ready-made 8pt grid scale here.

The rules file is what the agent reads before it touches any UI. The trick is to only write rules you could verify by reading the code. "Make it feel clean and modern" can't be checked. "No hex values outside tokens.css" can.

That last section matters more than it looks. Without it, the agent quietly invents a value whenever it can't find one. With it, it picks the nearest token and tells you, and you decide whether the system really needs something new.

Keep the file short, too. Anthropic's docs suggest keeping each CLAUDE.md under about 200 lines because longer files get followed less reliably, and Cursor's docs suggest under 500 lines per rule.

Each tool looks for instructions in its own place, which is where most setups get messy:

So put the rules in AGENTS.md, and make your CLAUDE.md this:

That's it. One file to edit, every tool reading the same rules.

If you'd rather not load design rules during backend work, both tools can scope a rule to certain files. In Cursor that's a .cursor/rules/design-system.mdc file with frontmatter:

In Claude Code it's a file in .claude/rules/ with a paths list at the top:

A rules file is a request. To know whether it worked, give the agent a prompt with plenty of design decisions in it ("a pricing section with three plans and a highlighted middle one" works well), then search the result.

Hard-coded colors outside your token file:

Empty output means the agent stayed inside the system. Anything else shows you exactly where it didn't, and usually there's a pattern, like the same missing grey turning up again and again. That tells you whether to tighten a rule or add a token. Once the searches pass a few times, put them in a pre-commit hook.

Grep won't catch everything, so still look at the page. Tab through it to check focus states, switch to dark mode, and watch for the sneaky case where every value is a token but the wrong one, like muted text on a main heading.

You'll see DESIGN.md mentioned a lot lately. It's an open format from Google Labs (still alpha): YAML tokens at the top, plain-language reasoning below. It's good for the why behind your system, like "primary is for one action per screen".

Two cautions. Not every tool picks it up automatically, so reference it from your rules file or add @DESIGN.md to CLAUDE.md. And don't keep the same hex values in DESIGN.md and your CSS. Two copies of a value will disagree eventually.

This is where a working setup usually breaks. If you rename --color-brand to --color-primary in CSS but not in the rules file, the agent keeps reaching for a token that's gone. Change both in the same commit, keep the old name as an alias (--color-brand: var(--color-primary);) until a search shows nothing uses it, and start a fresh chat after big changes.

Writing all of this from scratch takes a while, which is why I built the export into Constrainly. Each builder exports your tokens as CSS or a Tailwind v4/v3 theme, plus a matching rules file saved as AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md or a ready-made .cursor/rules file with the frontmatter already in place. The Design Language Studio does colors, type, spacing, radius and shadows in one pass and exports a single rules file for all of it. It's free and there's no account.

I've put a longer version of this guide, with more on Tailwind, hover and dark mode tokens, and keeping rules short, on my site: How to Make Cursor and Claude Code Follow Your Design System.

If you've found other rules that make a real difference with your agent, I'd like to hear them in the comments.

Gửi Xem: quay màn hình ngay trên trình duyệt, gửi một đường link là xong

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Mình là người làm ra Gửi Xem (guixem.vn), nên bài này là giới thiệu sản phẩm của chính mình. Mình muốn nghe góp ý từ những người hay phải giải thích bằng hình: báo lỗi cho đồng nghiệp, hướng dẫn khách hàng, làm tài liệu nội bộ.

Mở trang, bấm quay, chọn màn hình hoặc cửa sổ cần ghi. Quay xong bạn nhận ngay một đường link để dán vào Zalo, Telegram hay email. Người nhận mở link là xem được, không cần cài gì.

Thử tại guixem.vn và cho mình biết cảm nhận nhé. Cảm ơn bạn đã đọc.

Labeling AI content for Russia's March 2027 rules: an engineering checklist

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 11 • Originally published at ai-release.org

Russian rules on labeling AI-generated content take effect on 1 March 2027. The legal analysis belongs to someone else; what landed on my desk is the engineering side — making sure every asset that leaves the pipeline carries a provenance record both a person and a machine can read, and that it cannot quietly lose that record later. Below is the setup I would hand to another developer.

Budget 90–120 minutes for the first asset. Once the manifest format, the metadata commands and the CI script exist, each additional asset costs roughly ten minutes.

Rule number one: every artifact gets one manifest and one asset file sharing the same ID. That single convention is what makes everything else scriptable — checksums, metadata writing and the CI loop all key off the same name.

Tools to have ready, with the version check I run first:

The manifest is a plain-text provenance record: what produced the asset, when, and whether a human reviewed it. It exists before any output surface, so the file metadata, the page label and the API header are all derived from one source and cannot drift apart. Writing it first also forces you to capture the model identifier while you still have it — copy the exact string from the API response body, never type a model name from memory.

For AI-written text that an editor reworked, keep the same schema and set origin to ai-assisted.

Describe the fields in schema/label.schema.json and make these required: asset_id, asset_sha256, created_at, origin, model_id, ai_generated_share, human_reviewed, disclosure. Then hash the file and validate:

Confirm the schema actually enforces something: the terminal should print manifest valid. Then copy the manifest, remove model_id, validate the copy, and check that you get a ValidationError naming that field. A schema that never fails is decoration.

Three different readers need three different surfaces. Metadata inside the file survives download and re-upload. The visible label is what a human sees. An HTTP header is what an automated client sees. A caption alone vanishes the moment someone embeds the image elsewhere, and metadata alone is invisible to a reader — ship both.

Image metadata with ExifTool (-overwrite_original keeps .jpg_original backups out of your build output):

For video, -codec copy remuxes without re-encoding, so quality is untouched and the job finishes in seconds:

Page markup — one tag for machines, one sentence for people:

And on every API endpoint that returns generated text, image or audio:

If your provider supports embedded, signed provenance manifests, sign the file with c2patool instead of plain XMP. Flag names and signing-key setup change between releases, so read the current docs and pin the version you used.

Check both surfaces: exiftool -s3 -XMP-dc:Description assets/img-2027-0001.jpg should print the description line, and curl -sI https:///api/render | grep -i x-ai-generated should return x-ai-generated: true.

Labels disappear quietly. Design tools, image optimizers and CDN pipelines strip XMP blocks without complaint, and nobody notices until someone else does. A failing build is the only reminder that survives a team change.

Wire it into CI as a single step running bash scripts/verify_labels.sh, and make that step required for merges.

Check it end to end: three lines should appear in order — manifests/img-2027-0001.json valid, assets/img-2027-0001.jpg: OK, all labels verified. Then copy an asset, strip it with exiftool -all= assets/test-unlabeled.jpg, re-run the script and confirm you see MISSING LABEL: assets/test-unlabeled.jpg followed by echo $? printing 1.

Scope is a legal question, not a build question. Which of your outputs fall under the rules, which exemptions apply, and the exact wording of the disclosure all have to be confirmed against the current regulation text with your legal team before you ship anything. Everything above covers only the mechanics of producing and enforcing the label.

This article was originally published on AI Release — https://ai-release.org. Shorter, recurring notes on tooling and compliance work like this show up in the channel at https://t.me/ai_release1.

Can your Odoo automations trigger each other forever?

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

With enough automated actions, one of them will eventually write a field that another one watches. Odoo's automation module defines exactly when that happens: create triggers, write triggers, and special triggers (stage, state, priority, user, tags, archive) that watch a specific field.

Disclosure: I make a paid Odoo module that draws this map (link at the bottom).

That means the chain is computable. For each automation, look at what its actions write (object-write actions give a field path, Python actions can be read for write({...}), record.field = ... and env[...].create), and link it to every active automation that listens to that model and field. Strongly connected groups are loops, and two automations writing the same field on the same event is a conflict.

I built a module that draws that map, grouped by model, and flags loops, conflicts, rules with no action, code that no longer compiles, and schedules that keep failing. It's called Automation Map, 39 EUR on the Apps Store: https://apps.odoo.com/apps/modules/19.0/automation_map/

It reads Python statically, so code that builds model or field names dynamically can't be followed.

GTA 6 is causing internet issues as players rush to preload

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The demand for GTA 6 is already putting a strain on internet providers, with one UK company reporting widespread issues as players rush to preload.

After years of waiting, Rockstar’s next Grand Theft Auto is finally set to arrive on November 19, and players are already preparing for launch by preloading the game ahead of time.

However, the sheer number of people trying to download GTA 6 at once appears to be causing problems, even for those who aren’t planning to play it.

UK internet provider YouFibre warned customers that the surge in downloads was affecting its network, resulting in slower internet speeds.

In an email sent to customers, YouFibre confirmed that GTA 6 was responsible for the disruption.

“We are aware of a network capacity issue affecting the YouFibre network, due to the high demand of GTA 6 downloads,” the company explained. “Unfortunately, this has caused the overall download speeds of the service to decrease.”

Meanwhile, YouFibre CEO Jeremy Chelot also addressed the situation on X, revealing that parts of Yorkshire were experiencing problems.

“GTA6 is causing issue with one of our [Broadband Network Gateways]…trying to rebalance the traffic…therefore people around Yorkshire are currently suffering congestion… bear with us please,” he wrote.

GTA6 is causing issue with one of our BNG…trying to rebalance the traffic therefore people around Yorkshire are currently suffering congestion…bear with us please.

Fortunately, the disruption didn’t last long, with YouFibre later sending a second email confirming that the emergency work had been completed.

“Our Network Team have completed the emergency traffic management to spread the load of the network surge we had this evening across the wider network, and the service has returned to normal,” it said.

GTA 6 Online files discovered and show major changes from GTA 5

Spectrum offers free GTA 6 internet upgrade to download massive 212GB game in under 30 minutes

Netflix goes down as millions tune in for GTA 6 extended look

However, the company warned that those actively downloading GTA 6 could still notice slower speeds, as the game download was being prioritized.

With GTA 6’s long-awaited release not too far away, this is an early indication of just how popular the game is expected to be, with many predicting it will be a record-breaking success.

Precipio (PRPO) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Precipio targets a $40 million run rate by 2027 as it expands its sales team.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Precipio, Inc. (PRPO +6.53%) reported third-quarter results characterized by a transition from achieving financial independence in 2025 toward a strategy of accelerated revenue growth for 2027. Management reported that the company achieved a quarterly cash balance exceeding $3.5 million while maintaining positive adjusted EBITDA and operating cash flow. The company stated its intent to leverage established distribution agreements with four major industry partners to scale its product division, targeting a combined annualized revenue run rate of $40 million by the end of 2027. This growth plan includes doubling the sales force and hiring a senior marketing executive to enhance clinical credibility and market awareness. Management noted that current facility infrastructure is already equipped to handle up to $100 million in annual revenue without further major capital expenditures.

Now let me hand the call over to Ilan Danieli, Precipio's CEO. Please go ahead.

Ilan Danieli: Good afternoon, everyone, and thank you for joining us. Before we jump into a review of the results for Q3 2026 and share several thoughts for 2027, I thought that as we come to the end of another successful year, it might be helpful to add another member of the management team to these calls. I'd like to introduce my Co-Founder and Precipio's Chief Operating Officer, Zaki Sabet. Along with Dr. Ayman Mohamed, the 3 of us started Precipio in 2011, and we've been inseparable since. Prior to founding Precipio, Zaki spent his career in the diagnostics industry. While at college, Zaki worked as an accessioner during the night shift in a lab in New Jersey.

And over the years, he moved up through the ranks of lab tech then supervisor to eventually manage the entire laboratory. Bringing his in-depth expertise, Zaki was responsible for building what is today, to the best of our knowledge, at our size, one of the few profitable hematopathology laboratories in the country. At Precipio, Zaki oversees all company operations, including both our clinical laboratory as well as operations for the products division, including manufacturing and supply chain. Today, Zaki will be joining me in sharing some remarks, and then we'll also be available to answer questions during the Q&A section.

As this is our last scheduled shareholder call of 2026, I'd like to put these results in the context of the progress we've made over the past couple of years and explain how that progress sets the stage for 2027. In 2025, our focus was on getting the company to a cash flow positive position. We've gone through several dilutive financings and decided that it was time for the company to stand on its own 2 feet and become financially independent. So we set our eyes on reaching cash flow positive through a combination of revenue growth alongside significant operational efficiency improvements. And at the end of 2025, we were there.

Our company was cash flow positive, no longer dependent on outside capital in order to survive. And for the first time, we were able to respond to the calls from the various investment banks and hedge funds looking to provide capital to Precipio with a polite but clear, no thank you. I can tell you, as the person responsible for raising money to keep the lights on for 15 years, that was a wonderful moment. The second goal in 2025 was to determine if the products we created and that were used in our own lab would be real value to other laboratories.

We carefully explored this market by identifying target customers in several segments, which would serve as an indicator of market interest. This meant reaching a better understanding of which products in which configuration suited each segment. It meant continuously refining our sales presentation and value proposition to give the customer what they wanted and also to determine which level of support was required by each customer.

While many investors have observed that our revenue in 2025 remained relatively flat, our learning experience was a critical step at that time and serves as an important foundation for us to determine the course for our sales team to efficiently target the market, for our R&D team to build the right products and for our support team to be well equipped to onboard our customers. At the end of 2025, we had accomplished 2 key tasks: first, financial independence; and second, a market understanding of our customers and our value proposition.

In 2026, with a better understanding of our customer base, our product mix and how we can best serve our customer, we set out to validate our go-to-market strategy, reaching customers via distribution. One of the things we learned in 2025 and before was that in the laboratory space, getting in front of the customer and meeting the right decision-makers is really difficult. And post-COVID, if you didn't have a personal relationship, it was near impossible. We also learned that once we were in front of the decision-maker, we were quite successful in making the sale. So how do we get that door open for us? The distributors hold an important role in this field.

Working with the top 4 distributors in the industry, Thermo Fisher, Medline, McKesson and Cardinal Health, we have learned the power of the 200-plus sales reps each of the distributors employ. These are armies of well-trained, experienced and well-connected reps that have a long-established relationship with their customer, often spanning 10 to 15 years. There is no amount of money that would enable us to replicate even a fraction of that sales force power. And so we decided that distribution was the way to go. We also learned that each distributor is different, with different organizational structures, divisional responsibilities between different reps and organizational culture differences that dictate how best to interact with each distributor.

In 2026, we set out to figure the recipe for success with each distributor. During this process, we also learned what were the internal skill sets we needed to develop within our commercial team to best apply each recipe with each distributor. As we stand now at the end of 2026, we feel we figured out most of what we set out to discover in terms of the recipe with each distributor. That has led to some new hires in our commercial team and to a clear internal articulation of our go-to-market strategy via distribution. You've seen the gradual growth of the product division over the first 3 quarters of this year.

While it's always nice to see the business grow, this is by no means satisfactory to us nor it is a reflection of our planned growth trajectory going forward, quite the contrary. Precipio will -- 2027 will mark an important next phase in Precipio's evolution as we shift our focus from developing and validating our commercial strategy to accelerated growth. With the right products, a clear understanding of our target customers and established distribution strategy and the commercial infrastructure we're putting in place, we believe we now have the foundation to pursue a significantly higher rate of growth in our products business.

For the first time, we'd like to share with shareholders some specific financial objectives against which we intend to measure our progress. For our product division, we are targeting an annual revenue run rate exceeding $10 million by the end of 2027 compared with approximately $4 million annualized run rate based on our third quarter results of 2026. Our pathology business will also remain an important part of our growth strategy. Beyond its role in supporting product development and clinical validation, it also continues to generate the majority of our operating cash flow. We intend to continue growing this business, targeting an annualized revenue run rate of approximately $30 million by the end of 2027.

Together, these objectives represent a combined annualized revenue run rate exceeding $40 million by the end of 2027. To be clear, these are targets for the annualized revenue run rate we aim to achieve as we exit 2027 rather than a forecast of total revenue recognized during the year. We believe achieving these objectives would represent a significant milestone for Precipio, demonstrating the scalability of our products business while strengthening the financial foundation that enables us to fund continuing growth, product innovation and expansion into new diagnostic markets.

We recognize that these are ambitious objectives and delivering against them will require disciplined execution, but we believe the progress made over the past 2 years positions us well for this next phase of growth. Now let's turn to a review of Q3. Total revenue increased from approximately $7 million in Q2 to $7.3 million in Q3. Pathology revenue was approximately $6.3 million and product revenue exceeded, for the first time, $1 million a quarter, an exciting milestone. Adjusted EBITDA was approximately $400,000, consistent with Q2. Our operating cash flow was approximately $600,000 compared with approximately $700,000 in the second quarter.

Total cash increased by approximately $0.5 million during Q3, bringing our quarter end cash balance above $3.5 million compared with approximately $2.3 million a year earlier. These results give us a stronger financial foundation. With the confidence of a consistently cash-flowing business, it's now time to shift the balance. While maintaining positive cash flow, we plan to shift our focus to significant accelerated revenue growth. So let's discuss how we plan to scale up for growth on the product side. 2026 is all about understanding the recipe for growth.

As we discussed, we view distribution as the key source of scalability for our products business, and we wanted to understand how to best make that channel work before committing resources to a broader commercial expansion. We deliberately approached this with a relatively small commercial team and a measured level of spending. We wanted to refine the distributor model and our overall sales strategy before building a larger sales and marketing effort around them. The sequence was intentional: establish the approach, learn from execution and then invest in scaling it. We now believe we have a much clearer understanding of that recipe and how to support it.

Our ability to connect effectively with the distributor reps, arm them with information to get us a meeting and proceed to build a pipeline, all give us confidence we're ready for the next stage. It's now time to shift our emphasis from developing the model and understanding the recipe to implementing and expanding its reach. In 2027, growth will be our central commercial priority. We intend to significantly expand the commercial team and set aggressive goals for them while increasing marketing and technical support behind our products. It's time to pour gas on the fire, utilizing the incoming cash flow as resources towards accelerating the distributor and sales approach we spent this year developing.

An important part of that plan is bringing in a senior marketing executive to lead initiatives that were not previously pursued at this level. We want a more active presence in the market and a more systemic way to build awareness, credibility and access to prospective customers. That includes a stronger presence at relevant conferences, where we can engage laboratories, clinicians and our distribution partners. It also includes building a network of key opinion leaders who can provide clinical insights and help inform the evidence needed to support adoption. We plan to build clinical credibility through additional publications and case studies that explain the application and value of our technologies.

Our products need to be supported with evidence useful to the physicians and laboratories making testing decisions. Marketing will also work closely with sales on prospecting and customer access. The goal is to identify the right accounts, reach the people involved in testing decisions and give our sales team stronger materials and more opportunities to substantive customer discussions. That should extend to our distributors as well as to our direct commercial efforts. These initiatives are intended to work together. Conferences create opportunities for engagements, clinical relationships and evidence, strengthen our credibility and coordinated marketing and sales follow-up help move that interest towards evaluation and adoption.

Since quarter end, we have made changes to the commercial leadership and are bringing our products and pathology sales efforts under a more coordinated structure. Our recent product training week brought commercial, clinical and operational teams together to deepen product knowledge and strengthen customer conversations. These steps all support the broader growth effort we are preparing for next year. We will measure progress through customers completing implementation, beginning clinical use and placing repeat orders, ultimately resulting in revenue growth. Aggressive goals need to translate into results, and we intend to maintain that discipline and focus as we expand our commercial activity.

As we move from developing our commercial strategy to executing against a much more ambitious growth plan, operational leadership and accountability across the entire organization will become increasingly important. Zaki has been instrumental in building the infrastructure that brought us to this point. So now I've asked him to join this call and share his perspective on how we plan to scale the business from here. Zaki, all yours.

Ahmed Sabet: Thank you, Ilan, and good afternoon, everyone. As Ilan has outlined, we've spent the last several years building the foundation for Precipio's next stage of growth. My focus has been on developing an organization that not only can deliver high-quality diagnostic services and products, but to do so efficiently, profitably and at a significantly greater scale than where we operate today. I believe one of Precipio's greatest strengths is the operational infrastructure we've already built, and the opportunity we now have to leverage that infrastructure as the business grows. Within our pathology division, we've demonstrated our ability to grow revenue while improving efficiency and profitability.

We've invested in our people, systems, processes and laboratory capabilities, creating an operating model that we believe can support substantial additional volume without requiring a corresponding increase in our cost base. On the product side, we applied that same philosophy. We've built manufacturing capabilities, quality systems and technical support infrastructure designed not just for our current level of sales, but for the much larger business we intend to build. And importantly, these 2 divisions are deeply connected. Our clinical laboratory is not simply a source of revenue and cash flow.

It is also where we develop, validate and continuously improve our technologies, giving us a unique understanding of how our products perform in real-world clinical environments, and what our customers need to implement them successfully. To put the scale of the opportunity into perspective, we estimate that our existing operational infrastructure has the capacity to support approximately $100 million in annual revenue, roughly equally divided between pathology services and product manufacturing without requiring significant additional capital investment in our core facilities and equipment. That is a substantial amount of capacity relative to where our business stands today, and it creates an important opportunity for us to scale revenue, improve margins and drive greater profitability as we grow.

Ilan Danieli: Thank you, Zaki. In summary, the progression is clear. In 2025, we focused on reaching positive cash flow and financial independence. In 2026, as we generated cash, we studied and refined the commercial approach we believed can scale our products business. In 2027, we intend to put significant resources directly towards implementing that commercial approach and focus entirely on growth. We enter the next year with a strong financial foundation and a clear commercial direction. Our task is now to turn that preparation into faster growth. I'd like to thank our dedicated team of employees for their work and to our shareholders for your continued support. 2027 is going to be another pivotal year for us.

Zaki and I will now take your questions. Thank you.

Ilan Danieli: Yes. Thank you. Great question. Currently, our sales force is 4, and we are planning on doubling that next year. So we're going to expand that significantly. We expect by the beginning of the year to hire probably 2 more folks at least, and then continue throughout the year to really build this up.

Part of it is also to meet the demand from our distributors as we strengthen our connections with them, we'll get more motivated, more and more prospects, more and more meetings that we need to handle.

Ilan Danieli: Yes. That's -- unfortunately, that's still an unknown variable which should be broad range. GrowGeneration (GRWG) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Store cuts and tariff refunds drove a profit swing as margins climbed sharply.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

GrowGeneration Corp. (GRWG -3.82%) reported a transition from a retail-centric hydroponic provider to a national commercial cultivation supply and proprietary brand platform. Management reduced its retail footprint to 19 stores during the third quarter and expects to operate 14 locations by the end of 2026, down from 65 locations in 2022. The company achieved its highest quarterly net income since 2021, supported by $2.6 million in tariff refunds and increased sales of internal brands. While total revenue declined slightly compared to the prior year, the company reported positive adjusted EBITDA for the second consecutive quarter and maintained a debt-free balance sheet with $41.8 million in liquidity.

Operator: Hello, everyone, and welcome to GrowGeneration's Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Conference Call. My name is Julie, and I will be your operator for today's call. [Operator Instructions] This conference call is being recorded, and a replay of today's call will be available on the Investor Relations section of GrowGeneration's website. I will now hand the call over to Phil Carlson with KCSA Strategic Communications for introductions and the reading of the safe harbor statements. Please go ahead, Phil.

Phil Carlson: Thank you, operator, and welcome, everyone, to GrowGeneration's Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Results Conference Call. With us today from GrowGeneration are Darren Lampert, Co-Founder and Chief Executive Officer; and Greg Sanders, Chief Financial Officer. The company's third quarter 2026 earnings press release was issued after close of market today. A copy of this press release is available on the Investor Relations section of the GrowGeneration website at ir.growgeneration.com. I would like to remind everyone that certain comments made on this call include forward-looking statements, which are subject to the safe harbor provisions of the Private Securities Litigation Reform Act of 1995.

These forward-looking statements are based on management's current expectations and beliefs concerning future events and are subject to several risks and uncertainties that could cause actual results to differ materially from those described in these forward-looking statements. SEC filing as well as the earnings press release, which provide reconciliations of non-GAAP financial measures to the most directly comparable GAAP measures are all available on our website.

Following prepared remarks, management will be happy to take your questions. [Operator Instructions]. Now I will hand the call over to GrowGeneration's Co-Founder and CEO, Darren Lampert. Darren, please go ahead.

Darren Lampert: Thanks, Phil, and good afternoon, everyone. Thank you for joining us to review GrowGeneration's third quarter 2026 financial results and to discuss our outlook for the remainder of the year. The third quarter represents another step in GrowGeneration's transformation into a commercial proprietary brand-driven business. We delivered net sales of $44.8 million, gross margins of 32.4% and $2.3 million of adjusted EBITDA. These results demonstrate our progress in fundamentally reshaping our business, strengthening our revenue quality and building a more efficient and profitable operating model. Over the past several years, we've expanded beyond our retail roots to serve commercial growers nationwide.

Three priorities have guided us, expanding our commercial sales team, growing sales of our proprietary brands and lowering the cost of serving customers by making better use of our distribution network. At the center of our transformation is the expansion of our commercial business. GrowGeneration has developed a national platform serving commercial cultivators across specialty agriculture and controlled environmental agriculture. Our competitive advantage is our cultivation expertise and a comprehensive portfolio of cultivation supplies. Proprietary brand sales grew approximately 19% year-over-year to $14.5 million, while surpassing 40% of cultivation and gardening net revenue for the quarter as we continue to see strong growth from our portfolio of brands.

We've also continued to consolidate locations and simplify our distribution network to lower the cost of serving growers. In the third quarter, store and other operating expenses fell approximately 12% year-over-year from $7.2 million to $6.4 million. During the quarter, we began the process of closing 2 additional stores, which was completed subsequent to quarter end. We expect to close another 2 to 3 locations before year-end, bringing our total fourth quarter closures to approximately 4 to 5 locations. These actions, together with targeted workforce reductions and other operational improvements are part of our broader restructuring plan to reduce fixed costs and improve operating efficiency.

We expect to complete the majority of these initiatives by the end of 2026, positioning GrowGeneration for greater profitability in the year ahead. Technology is another way we're increasing productivity and lowering costs. GrowGen Pro already gives our commercial and wholesale customers a convenient way to manage their accounts and place orders online. In October, we acquired technology from Order Junky to improve our sales team's efficiency. We're integrating tools that bring customer pricing, inventory availability and freight costs into a single quote that a sales rep can turn into an order without reentering the details. We're also evaluating the platform's AI capabilities can reduce routine administrative work.

For 2027, our goal is to give growers faster, more accurate quotes and make repeat purchases easier. We expect this will free our sales team to spend more time with customers and help us grow commercial sales without a corresponding increase in overhead. Turning to MMI Storage Solutions. The business generated $9.2 million in third quarter revenue, ahead of our expectations. MMI's performance through September has exceeded our initial expectations for 2026, and we're on track for strong year-over-year revenue growth for the full year. Looking ahead, we see opportunities to put MMI's engineering and manufacturing capabilities to work for more customers in industrial and specialty markets, expanding our business beyond cultivation.

The progress we're making is increasingly visible in our profitability. For the 9 months ended September 30, adjusted EBITDA improved to approximately $1 million from a loss of $4 million a year ago. We also reported our highest GAAP net income quarter since 2021. While the third quarter tariff recovery contributed to that improvement, we're continuing to focus on the underlying initiatives that will continue to drive our business to greater levels of profitability. We ended the quarter with $41.8 million in cash, cash equivalents and marketable securities and no debt. That gives us flexibility to invest selectively in our commercial business, proprietary brands and technology.

During the quarter, we also returned approximately $1 million to shareholders through the repurchase of 700,000 shares at an average price of $1.46 per share. Given our progress to date and increased visibility, we are refining our full year revenue guidance to $163 million to $165 million from our original range of $162 million to $168 million. We're also taking further steps in the fourth quarter to strengthen profitability in 2027. With 4 to 5 additional store closures we plan to complete by year-end, along with other restructuring activities, we believe it is prudent to withdraw our previously full year adjusted EBITDA guidance.

And while these actions may create some near-term uncertainty, longer term, they will meaningfully lower our cost base and position the business for improved profitability in 2027. Looking ahead, our plan is to accelerate GrowGeneration's evolution into a leading national commercial cultivation supply and proprietary brand platform with multiple engines for profitable growth. To do that, we will prioritize expanding our proprietary brand penetration within our commercial customer base establishing deeper strategic relationships with commercial cultivators, expanding their operations and continuing to broaden MMI's customer base by entering additional verticals. Underlying all of this is our commitment to building a leaner, more efficient, technology-enabled organization.

We intend to leverage our national distribution infrastructure, commercial sales capabilities, proprietary brands and customer relationships to grow revenue while managing our cost structure. That concludes my remarks. Now I'll turn the call over to our CFO, Greg Sanders.

Greg Sanders: Thank you, Darren, and good afternoon, everyone. I'll begin with a review of our third quarter 2026 results, and then I'll provide additional context on our outlook for the rest of the year. We reported third quarter results that were consistent with our expectations and reflect the continuing transformation of GrowGeneration. Our results were characterized by strong proprietary brand sales in our cultivation and Gardening segment and continued growth and profitability in our Storage Solutions business. We delivered another quarter of positive adjusted EBITDA as well as a substantial year-over-year improvement in net income as we reported positive net income for the quarter.

For the third quarter of 2026, GrowGeneration reported net sales of $44.8 million compared to $47.3 million during the same period last year. Our revenue was in line with our guidance range and a slight decrease to 2025 due to a few large onetime transactions in the prior year period. Cultivation and Gardening segment net sales were $35.6 million for the quarter compared to $38.4 million in the same period last year. Proprietary brand sales represented 40.6% of Cultivation and Gardening revenue, up from 31.6% last year. Proprietary brand sales increased $2.3 million or 19.3% to $14.4 million for the quarter.

Through September, those sales grew $7.6 million or 23.4% to $40.1 million, offsetting the decline in third-party product sales and keeping total segment revenue slightly ahead of last year. Storage Solutions net sales were $9.2 million in the third quarter, up from $8.9 million a year ago and ahead of our expectations. Demand from retail and industrial customers contributed to the segment's performance. For the first 9 months of 2026, sales increased $2.2 million or 10.2% to $24 million, putting MMI ahead of our initial plan for the year. Gross profit increased $14.5 million compared to $12.9 million a year ago, and gross margin rose 520 basis points to 32.4% from 27.2%.

As anticipated, we recognized a $2.6 million benefit from IEEPA tariff refunds as a reduction in cost of sales. Cultivation and Gardening accounted for the increase in gross profit with segment gross profit up $1.7 million or 18.4%. In addition to the IEEPA refunds, a higher mix of proprietary brand sales helped margin. Lower sales from a smaller footprint for our stores weighed on gross profit, while pricing pressure, higher transportation costs and higher estimated inventory obsolescence costs weighed on margin. In Storage Solutions, gross profit dollars were essentially unchanged, although segment margin declined modestly. Turning to expenses. Total operating expenses declined 7.1% to $14.6 million from $15.7 million a year ago.

Store and other operating expenses fell 11.9% to $6.4 million from $7.2 million, reflecting our smaller store footprint. SG&A increased to $6.8 million from $5.7 million, driven by higher commercial sales-related spending and one-time charges incurred during the quarter. Depreciation and amortization totaled $1.2 million, down $1.5 million or 55.2% compared to $2.6 million in the same period last year. The decrease primarily reflects asset retirements related to cost reduction initiatives and certain intangible assets reaching the end of their useful lives. GAAP net income was $0.2 million, a $2.7 million improvement compared to a net loss of $2.4 million or negative $0.04 per share in the prior year period.

The improvement was primarily driven by higher gross margin, including the tariff refund benefit and lower operating expenses, partially offset by lower sales volume. In the third quarter, we delivered our second consecutive quarter of positive adjusted EBITDA. Non-GAAP adjusted EBITDA, as defined in our press release, was $2.3 million, a $1 million year-over-year improvement compared to $1.3 million in the prior year. Now turning to the balance sheet. We ended the quarter with $41.8 million of cash, cash equivalents and marketable securities and no debt. We continue to maintain a debt-free balance sheet. Our cash position gives us the financial flexibility to invest in organic growth, evaluate strategic opportunities and opportunistically return capital to shareholders.

We also returned approximately $1 million to shareholders during the quarter, repurchasing 700,000 shares at an average price of $1.46 per share, excluding transaction costs. We did so while preserving the liquidity we need to fund operations and invest in the business. At quarter end, approximately $8 million remained available for future repurchase. As Darren outlined, we are narrowing our full year revenue guidance to $163 million to $165 million. We are also withdrawing our previous adjusted EBITDA guidance of $2 million to $3 million due to the actions we are now taking to lower our ongoing cost base and position GrowGeneration for greater profitability in 2027.

While the store closures and other restructuring activities will affect our fourth quarter results, we expect the resulting savings and operating efficiencies to strengthen our financial performance in the year ahead. That said, we're satisfied with our third quarter results and with our performance through September, which was ahead of our initial plan for the year. We ended the quarter with $41.8 million in cash, cash equivalents, marketable securities and no debt. We have the financial flexibility to carry out our fourth quarter initiatives while continuing to invest in the business. We believe these actions position us to extend our multiyear track record of improving profitability heading into 2027.

With that, I'll turn the call back to Darren for closing remarks.

Darren Lampert: Thanks, Greg, and thank you again to everyone for joining us today. In closing, the most important takeaway today is that GrowGeneration is a fundamentally different business from what we were several years ago. We have substantially transitioned from a retail-centric hydroponic business into a national commercial cultivation supply and proprietary brand platform. We've expanded our proprietary brands, reduced operating expenses and improved profitability. And we've accomplished this while maintaining a strong balance sheet with no debt. Our third quarter results demonstrate the progress we've already made to building a stronger, more profitable company that generates sustainable cash flow and creates long-term value for our shareholders. I want to thank our employees, customers, vendors and stakeholders for their continued support.

That concludes our prepared remarks. Operator, please open the line for questions

Greg Sanders: Yes. Thanks for the question, Aaron. So far, we're encouraged with the performance in 2026. Aaron Grey: Okay. So if not able to quantify now, I guess, maybe taking it a different way, the stores that you are closing, the 4 to 5, those were weighing on the margin now. Darren Lampert: I think, Aaron, when you take a look at it, this will be -- we've closed 10 stores since the third quarter last year. Adding the additional 4 stores in the fourth quarter will be down 14 stores from Q3 last year. Each store closing certainly goes with cost savings, both from store employees, lease, and it funnels down to back-office staff and everything that goes into running these stores. When you start taking a look at the EBITDA guidance that we're giving where we started a couple of years ago, back in 2024, we lost about $16.5 million on an adjusted basis.

What we learned from Round 2 of AHSAA flag football playoffs in the Montgomery area

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Montgomery area saw success when it came to area high school flag football teams in the second round of the AHSAA flag football playoffs.

The River Region had 12 teams playing Round 2 games across the four AHSAA classifications, and 10 of those teams advanced to either the quarterfinal or semifinal rounds in their respective brackets. In the Class 1-4A bracket, four of those teams will meet in the quarterfinal round, guaranteeing that a team from the Montgomery area will make it to the state championship game in Mobile.

For the most part, it was a dominate night for Montgomery-area teams, with a couple of thrillers mixed in.

The Montgomery Advertiser is here to provide analysis and context to Round 2 of the AHSAA flag football playoffs in the Montgomery area, and what it could mean heading into the quarterfinal and semifinal rounds.

It's a shame Montgomery Catholic will only compete for a private school title. The Knights have been one of the best teams in the state in the 2026 season. Besides a loss to Central-Phenix City in the season-opener, Montgomery Catholic has won 17 consecutive games against great talent from Class 1-6A.

The Knights earned a first round bye, then defeated Mobile Christian 48-13 in the quarterfinal round of the Single A-Double A bracket.

Montgomery Catholic finished as runner-up in the 2025 AHSAA 1-5A state championship game. The Knights were favorites back then and are consensus favorites to win the private school championship, in large part because it kept almost all of its roster from 2025.

There's only been one team that's gotten within one score of Montgomery Catholic. It's looking like their title to lose at this point.

One side of the bracket for the upcoming quarterfinal round will be nothing but River Region teams. It guarantees that the semifinal game will be between two Montgomery-area teams, also guaranteeing that a River Region team will represent in the state championship game in Mobile.

The question now is who will earn the right to punch their ticket to the title game?

The early front runner would be Brewbaker Tech. The Rams made it to the 1-5A semifinals last season before losing to Montgomery Catholic. The Rams have had a dominate season, with its only three losses coming at the hands of teams above them in classification.

Looking to challenge the Rams looks like it could be Tallassee. The Tigers have also enjoyed a successful season and are no stranger to the playoffs. But don't give up on Marbury to make a push. The Bulldogs had issues with Brewbaker Tech in the regular season, but are coming off a thrilling fourth quarter win over Selma in the second round.

Stanhope Elmore, Wetumpka and Prattville have all enjoyed successful seasons. The trio all advanced to the quarterfinal rounds in Class 5A and 6A respectively.

Prattville will draw the toughest matchup in the quarterfinals, having to go against reigning national champion Central-Phenix City. The Lions struggled to keep pace with the Red Devils during the regular season.

As for Wetumpka and Stanhope Elmore, the pair have interesting matchups that are winnable for both teams. If the two can survive the quarterfinal round, it'll setup a semifinal meeting between the two — guaranteeing another Montgomery-area team making it to the state championship in Class 5A.

Jaeden Day covers high school sports, Alabama State football and Auburn recruiting for the Montgomery Advertiser/USA Today. You can find him on X at dayjaeden12, or reach him at JDay@montgome.gannett.com.

Are Dabo Swinney, Clemson better in 2026? ACC Championship scenarios, bowl projections

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

CLEMSON — Clemson football achieved bowl eligibility by defeating Syracuse on Nov. 7. But at Clemson, success is rarely defined by just reaching a bowl game.

Tigers coach Dabo Swinney has become a victim of his own standards. He built Clemson to be a force in college football and won national championships in 2016 and 2018. Recently, though, the Tigers have slowly fallen from the top.

The 2026 season started with Swinney's worst loss as Clemson's coach in a 51-10 beatdown vs LSU, but it looks like the Tigers will finish stronger than last season's underwhelming 7-6 campaign. As Clemson goes deeper into the homestretch of its season, the question now becomes what kind of finish would allow its season to be viewed as a success.

Whether the Tigers ultimately reach the ACC Championship Game or land in one of the conference's top bowls, their final three games will determine whether this year was a step forward or another year out of the national conversation.

That starts with Clemson (6-3, 4-2 ACC) hosting Georgia Tech (2-7, 1-5) on Nov. 14 (12:30 p.m., The CW).

Clemson's postseason destination could influence how its season is viewed. The best the Tigers can finish is 9-3 by winning out against Georgia Tech, Duke and South Carolina.

Clemson should defeat the last-place Yellow Jackets, but Duke (7-2, 4-2 ACC) and surging No. 24 South Carolina (6-3) each look challenging. Neither team is LSU or Miami, though, and both are teams Clemson is capable of matching up with and defeating.

Finishing 9-3 would put the Tigers in position to secure one of the ACC's top bowl destinations like the Pop-Tarts Bowl or the Alamo Bowl. Making a bowl like that, and winning it, would be a strong finish to a season that couldn't have started more poorly.

Fans and national media may still view Clemson as falling behind after getting blown out by LSU and losing decisively to Miami. But a strong finish could show that the Tigers are moving in the right direction heading into 2027.

Clemson could also lose one or two games, creating more discontent about where the program and Swinney stand. Dropping games down the stretch and finishing 2026 on a sour note will leave the Tigers entering the offseason with more questions than answers.

More: How Sammy Brown keeps playing like a star linebacker for Dabo Swinney amid roller-coaster Clemson seasons

Clemson winning the next two games could mean a spot in the ACC Championship Game.

The Tigers are tied for fifth with Duke. Miami (6-0 ACC) is in first and Wake Forest (5-1) is in second ahead of Louisville and SMU (both 5-2).

Miami hosts Duke, Virginia Tech (3-3 ACC) and Boston College (2-4), so the Hurricanes losing more than one game seems unlikely.

The Demon Deacons control their own destiny, meaning Clemson would need them to lose at least one more game. Wake Forest plays at SMU, at Georgia Tech and at home vs. Duke.

The Tigers will play only eight conference games while others play nine, but they won't be punished under the ACC's new tiebreaking rules if multiple teams finish with two conference losses. A team that plays one more conference game than another won't have an advantage if they have the same number of conference losses. A head-to-head result is the first tiebreaker before shifting to records against common opponents.

The 2026 ACC Championship Game will be the last, as College Football Playoff expansion to 24 teams will eliminate conference title games in favor of an extra playoff round starting in 2027.

ESPN (Mark Schlabach): Sun Bowl vs. Washington (El Paso, Texas)

The Athletic: Holiday Bowl vs. Arizona (San Diego, California)

USA TODAY: Gator Bowl vs. Oklahoma (Jacksonville, Florida)

Sports Illustrated: Sun Bowl vs. Washington

Warren Buffett's Successor, Greg Abel, Just Bought $9.6 Billion of the Oracle of Omaha's Favorite Stock (No, Not Alphabet!)

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Since mid-2018, Buffett and Abel have deployed roughly $92 billion of Berkshire Hathaway’s capital into a company near and dear to the Oracle of Omaha’s heart.

Few events garner more attention on Wall Street than Berkshire Hathaway's (BRKA +2.60%)(BRKB +1.41%) earnings reports. This is especially true now that Warren Buffett has retired and his successor, Greg Abel, is overseeing the company's day-to-day operations and its closely watched $359 billion investment portfolio.

While the September-ended quarter was more or less business as usual for the trillion-dollar conglomerate that Buffett built, with Berkshire recording $12.7 billion in operating earnings, another figure stole the show. Namely, a $9.6 billion purchase of the Oracle of Omaha's favorite stock by Abel.

The Oracle of Omaha's favorite stock is back on the menu. Image source: Getty Images.

Through the first half of 2026, Abel's headline buy was Google parent Alphabet (GOOGL -0.27%)(GOOG -0.38%). While Buffett initiated the position in Alphabet before his retirement, it was Abel who grew it into a top-three holding at one point. Berkshire's new boss has put tech on the menu in a way that wasn't seen under Buffett.

But it wasn't a tech purchase that raised eyebrows in the third quarter. In fact, you won't even find evidence of this purchase in Berkshire Hathaway's quarterly Form 13F, which should be filed on Nov. 16.

In Berkshire's third-quarter operating results, on the final page before the executive certifications, the company details Abel's purchase activity in the stock Buffett holds nearest and dearest to his heart... Berkshire Hathaway.

BREAKING: Berkshire Hathaway more than doubled its stock buybacks in Q3 2026, repurchasing $9.6 billion of its own shares. This brings total buybacks in 2026 to $14.4 billion, marking a sharp reversal after the company paused repurchases for nearly 22 months. Berkshire also revealed that it has been a net buyer of stocks in 2026, with net purchases hitting +$11.2 billion YTD. This compares to net stock sales of -$10.6 billion over the same period in 2025. Berkshire is deploying capital again.

Between July 1 and Sept. 30, Abel oversaw the purchase of 4,536 Class A shares (BRKA) and 12,384,959 Class B shares (BRKA), totaling approximately $9.6 billion. This more than doubles the $4.53 billion spent on share buybacks in the second quarter and brings cumulative share repurchases between Buffett and Abel since mid-2018 to approximately $92 billion.

As long as Berkshire Hathaway has at least $30 billion in combined cash, cash equivalents, and U.S. Treasury bills on its balance sheet -- it closed out September 2026 with $377.4 billion -- and Abel believes the company's shares are intrinsically cheap, buybacks can be made with no end date or ceiling.

Since Berkshire Hathaway doesn't pay a dividend, buybacks are a direct way to reward its shareholders (in case a greater-than-6,000,000% outperformance of the S&P 500 since the mid-1960s wasn't enough). Buybacks encourage the long-term investing ethos that Buffett values and can incrementally increase investors' ownership stakes over time.

A hearty share repurchase program can also make a company's stock more attractive to fundamentally-focused investors. Companies with steady or growing net income and a declining outstanding share count, courtesy of an ongoing buyback program, should see earnings per share rise over time.

An aggressive share buyback program can also signal to Wall Street and investors that management sees value in their company's stock.

BRK.A Price to Book Value data by YCharts

Over Warren Buffett's final 19 months as CEO, as well as during Abel's first two months at the helm, not one penny was used for buybacks. During this 21-month period, Berkshire's stock predominantly traded at a 60% to 80% premium to its book value. Since mid-2018, Berkshire's bosses have consistently green-lit buybacks when its shares trade at less than a 50% premium to book value. For context, shares have oscillated between a 40% and 50% premium over the previous two quarters.

Even though Buffett and Abel have been decisive net sellers of stocks over the last four years, Buffett's favorite stock is clearly on the menu.

Nvidia's stock rose premarket on Wednesday, after falling for two days amid the scandal that has rocked the world's most valuable tech company.

Shares had closed down 5.47% on Tuesday as more details were revealed about the impact of CEO Jensen Huang's affair with an alleged Chinese spy. They fell 4.3% Monday.

Shares were 4.5% higher in premarket trading as of 5:00 a.m. ET.

On Tuesday, Nvidia announced it had hired law firm Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz to investigate whether company secrets or national security were compromised.

Huang tried to project "business as usual" as he returned to the office on Monday, people familiar with the matter told CNBC. The CEO was also in the office on Tuesday, conducting meetings, ahead of the company's quarterly earnings on Nov. 17.

Investors are assessing whether the matter will have damaging spillover effects on the business.

In a Tuesday note, Bank of America said it saw three scenarios with differing investment outcomes. In one scenario, the impact is contained with leadership continuity, and in another, prolonged uncertainty pressures valuation before earnings.

In the final scenario, the impact is escalated, with "adverse findings, leadership disruption or tighter restrictions requiring earnings reassessment," BofA research analyst Vivek Arya said in the note.

I’ve been using this gross hack to help unlock my phone as winter weather rolls in

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join the Tom's Guide Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Winter is coming at long last, after an extended and outrageously hot summer. Sadly that means the usual winter problems are coming with it, including cold air that inevitably causes my hands to dry out. Normally this is more of an annoyance than anything, but over the last few years it's started affecting my phone use.

Yes, that's right, cold weather has been interfering with how I use my phone. Specifically the under-display fingerprint scanner, a feature that is almost universally available on premium Android phones. It turns out, dry skin can affect whether those fingerprint scanners can recognize your digits. Thankfully, there's a useful hack that can help you out in a pinch — even if it is a little bit gross.

Obviously the best way to stop dry skin is to keep them moisturized, by whatever method or product you prefer. But I always forget about this until the effects of dry hands start to affect by day, at which point anything I do is reactive rather than preventative. That's no good in a pinch, but you know what is? Wiping your finger across your nose.

Yes, you read that correctly. It's a tip I first heard about back in 2023 after months of users reporting issues with the fingerprint sensor on the Pixel 6 and Pixel 7. Various users that experienced ongoing issues realized that dryness was the root cause of a lot of their problems, and somehow figured out that the oils in your face (which concentrate on your nose and forehead) were enough to briefly alleviate the issues.

At the time it was speculated that Google's use of the optical fingerprint scanner was a primary suspect in all the reported problems. Admittedly the ultrasonic fingerprint scanner that debuted on Pixel 9 works a lot better than the older scanners, but even that doesn't seem to be able to account for excess skin dryness that may occur during colder weather. Or, at least, that's been my experience using the Pixel 10 Pro.

The idea of using your own face grease to access your phone sounds gross, but the science behind it is pretty sound. Those oils on your skin act as a barrier to help prevent you from losing moisture.

I'm not sure whether it hydrates the skin directly, or if it just offers a small layer of moisture to facilitate the scanning process, but I'm not about to complain. Lotion apparently works too, assuming you tend to carry a bottle on your person at all time, but I haven't actually tested it myself. It's pretty rare that I go anywhere with my face fully covered these days, so the nose trick actually works pretty well for me.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

I first started noticing issues with my fingerprint scanner last week. My Pixel 10 Pro was straight up refusing to read my fingerprint, and failed so many times I had to resort to using my PIN to get into my phone. As simple as it is to type in a six digit number, it's nowhere near as convenient as slapping a finger or thumb onto the screen.

At first I wondered whether my screen protector was to blame. I dropped my phone onto a gravel path a couple of months back, chipping the edge of the protector and causing a crack to shoot across to the opposite side of the screen. Leaving a cracked protector for long enough means the damage inevitably spreads, and I assumed that this might have explained why my fingerprint scanner had suddenly stopped working 90% of the time.

Then I remembered the nose-touching hack, and figured it was worth a try. Lo and behold, the fingerprint scanner started working again. It wasn't back to 100%, but it was still enough to actually unlock my phone without hitting the fail limit and disabling biometrics in the process. I also discovered that touching my forehead, or anywhere else in the T-Zone, is just as effective.

In the short term, exploiting my own gross biology helped with unlocking my phone. In the long term, well, the only thing I can do is start moisturizing my hands more often and try to wear gloves when I'm doing work around the house.

Huawei unveils 'SuperCluster' architecture designed to make 1 million AI chips work as a single computer

The new Peerium architecture aims to power hyper-efficient computers capable of training AI models with up to 40 trillion parameters.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Engineers in China have built a new computer architecture they say can one day make up to a million artificial intelligence (AI) chips work as one. For comparison, Elon Musk's AI company, SpaceXAI (soon to be SpaceXSI), describes its Colossus cluster as the world's most powerful AI training system, comprising 200,000 interconnected chips.

Traditionally, the AI chips used to train foundation models are housed in separate servers and communicate over a network to share data and divide workloads. But the new architecture, Peerium, uses a high-speed interconnect system designed to maintain efficient communication between chips across multiple racks, even as the number of chips scales into the hundreds of thousands.

Huawei unveiled the new architecture Sept. 17 at the company's annual CONNECT conference.

Representatives from Huawei said in a statement that computers powered by Peerium could train models with up to 40 trillion parameters. For comparison, competing hardware, like GB200 NVL72 from NVIDIA and CS-3 from Cerebras, have reported training at the trillion-parameter scale, rather than the tens-of-trillions.

"Over the next two years, there will be roughly 6 or 7 frontier AI labs in China, and all will aim at training foundation language models of sizes ranging from 10 to 40 trillion parameters," Liao Heng, chief scientist for Huawei's chip design arm HiSilicon, told the Associated Press. "So you need such a scale of infrastructure to train these models."

Peerium's performance relies on three elements: nested parallelism, unified memory addressing, and peer-to-peer interconnects, Huawei representatives said in the statement.

Nested parallelism allows workloads to be broken into smaller tasks and organized across hierarchical groups of chips that work in parallel. It's similar to how a company breaks down work into departments and then smaller teams, all of which coordinate their efforts toward a common goal. Unified memory addressing allows processors to access memory across other servers, as opposed to only that attached to the processor's own server. Peer-to-peer interconnects allow components to communicate more directly with one another, rather than relying on an intermediary.

Get the world’s most fascinating discoveries delivered straight to your inbox.

All three principles are designed to make more efficient use of computing resources so that a single system can coordinate larger numbers of chips to handle demanding AI workloads.

According to Huawei, existing interconnect technologies — which link processors and servers to enable them to communicate — cannot support the Peerium architecture at the scale it envisages. For instance, NVIDIA uses NVLink for communication within racks and InfiniBand for communication between racks. The inter-rack communication bandwidth in this setup is 200GB per second, Eric Xu, Huawei's rotating chairman, told the AP.

"With such a low inter-rack communications bandwidth, there is no way you can make one million processors work as one computer," he said.

Huawei instead developed its own interconnect technology, UnifiedBus, which the company says delivers inter-rack communications bandwidth of up to 800GB per second.

"When one million processors work as one computer, this lays the foundation for better and more efficient AI training and inference," Xu added. "Therefore, I see UnifiedBus as the path for the future AI era. I believe, in the years to come, other players would certainly follow suit."

Alongside Peerium, Huawei also announced the Atlas 950 SuperPoD, a computing system built on Peerium and powered by more than 8,000 Huawei Ascend 950DT AI accelerator chips.

Xu said testing for AI training is underway on these SuperPoDs and that results show that they use their available computing power for AI training more efficiently than AI computing clusters that aren’t based on Peerium and UnifiedBus.

Huawei expects to begin supplying the SuperPoDs by early next year, at the latest, before its SuperClusters, which link multiple SuperPoDs to form a larger AI computing system, based on Peerium, eventually come online. Representatives said that one SuperCluster with 256,000 computing cards — hardware modules that add processing capacity — is currently being deployed and tested.

Models with large numbers of parameters demand greater computing resources and infrastructure, which isn't always desirable as it increases costs. In the summer, researchers used an IBM quantum computer to reduce the perplexity of Meta's Llama 3.1 8B language model — a measure of how well it predicts the next word or token — while keeping the number of parameters almost unchanged.

'No one dared question his results': How thermodynamics sparked a heated debate about Earth's age in the 19th century

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
