Revolut Pay arrives on Uber with RevPoints perks and Uber One access

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Customers can redeem RevPoints for instant discounts at checkout, and Premium members get Uber One benefits in 10 markets.

Revolut Pay is now available as a payment option on Uber, bringing the fintech’s checkout service into one of the world’s most widely used consumer apps.

The integration also links rides and deliveries paid through Revolut Pay to the company’s rewards scheme. Moreover, the company says millions of its customers use Uber every month.

The main incentive is RevPoints, as customers in the UK and European Economic Area will earn boosted points on every Uber ride and delivery paid for through Revolut Pay, with millions of users eligible.

The points can also be used at checkout. Customers can redeem RevPoints directly against the cost of a ride or order, or spend them on other offers in the Revolut app. That makes the loyalty scheme more immediately useful, effectively turning points into a discount on the next trip or delivery.

The service is available across the EU, except in Bulgaria, Cyprus, Latvia, and Slovenia. It also works in the UK, Switzerland, and Norway, for users aged 18 and over.

Revolut describes Uber as the latest addition to its growing network of global partners, saying it is “setting the new standard for how the world moves, spends, and earns”.

The partnership fits into Revolut’s broader effort to become more than a bank. The company completed a share sale valuing it at $115bn last week. Chief executive Nik Storonsky has also outlined a future in which AI agents shop online using customers’ cards.

A payment button inside Uber is a more immediate version of that ambition. Revolut provides the payment infrastructure while the customer stays inside another company’s app.

For riders, the appeal is straightforward: they can pay through a familiar service, collect points on trips and deliveries they would have made anyway, and use those points to reduce the cost of future orders.

For Revolut, each additional third-party checkout gives it another opportunity to keep customers using its payment services beyond its own app.

We’re integrating Revolut Pay in the app used by millions of Revolut customers each month.

Redeem RevPoints for discounts and unlock up to 15x RevPoints multiplier via Revolut Pay on @Uber & @UberEats until 6 January.

Leeds United winger Daniel James has conceded there is frustration that comes with a lack of playing time.

The jet-heeled wideman is rated highly by Whites boss Daniel Farke and has been a key figure throughout the German’s reign.

However, he has found opportunities limited this season and managed just one start in the Premier League.

James, now away on international duty with Wales, has discussed the importance of being a team player in an interview with the official Leeds website.

The 29-year-old said: “I think we've had a great start. I think it was very important for us to get a result coming to this international break. I thought we were really good, put on a great performance.

“But yeah, as a player, obviously, I can't lie, obviously you do get frustrated not playing, but you have to give your all for the team, because ultimately it's a team game and, even if you're not getting as many minutes as you want, you want to perform and train every day, push players that are starting as well.

He said: “I'm definitely just as quick. I think obviously I've had a few injuries and sometimes it's taken me a little bit longer [to recover], but I think, as I become more of a senior player, it's about how much you can maintain that, because I think it's not just about being quick once. You want to be able to do that the whole game.

"And I think as I've become more senior, I've learned to work around that a lot more. But it's something that I work on every day because, ultimately, I want to play, I want my career to be as long as possible. But I want to be as quick as long as possible.

“I think when you start getting 30 plus, I think it's about how much you can maintain it. But it's something that I'm always working on, and will do for the rest of my career.”

Score a month of T-Mobile 5G Home Internet and claim up to $200 back

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

An advertiser paid for editorial consideration of this deal. Our editorial experts vetted the deal based on our independent expertise. Because we determined that the deal will save money for our readers, we wrote the content.

T-Mobile has dropped an offer on its 5G Home Internet that's much needed ahead of the upcoming holiday season. This New Electric Paver Can Lay Down 1,200 Tonnes Of Asphalt A Day

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Browse this and many other websites without banner ads, personalized tracking and video ads.

The construction world is getting quieter by the day, thanks to the gradual electrification of big rigs traditionally powered by diesel engines. After trucks, diggers, and cranes, asphalt pavers are now going electric.

Switzerland’s Ammann, which is known for its asphalt and concrete plant equipment, has transformed one of its mainstream diesel asphalt pavers to run on electric power. Thanks to a Volvo Penta powertrain, the eABG 4820 is now one of the largest electric pavers on the market, capable of laying up to 500 tonnes per hour and a maximum output of 1,200 tonnes per day with a charging stop.

Without intermediate charging, the machine can put down between 350 and 450 tonnes of asphalt.

Before getting into the details, the biggest advantage of an electric paver over a diesel rig is the significantly lower noise output. A 99-kilowatt electric motor is powered by a massive 270–kilowatt-hour battery pack arranged in three separate units. Volvo Penta supplies the batteries, motors, power electronics, and control technology, while Ammann used its expertise in hydraulic-powered machinery to put it all together.

“It comes down to energy management and architecture,” said Philipp Bramer, head of the e-paver development team at Ammann. “The electric driveline doesn’t just power the machine. It communicates seamlessly with the hydraulics. Simultaneously, the intelligent control system makes sure that the system supplies exactly the right amount of energy to the machine.”

The nickel-cobalt-aluminum oxide (NCA) traction batteries can be charged from a CCS2 connector at up to 150 kW in roughly one and a half hours, while AC charging at up to 22 kW can replenish the battery in 12 hours. Amman says that the battery provides enough juice for an entire work shift, but that an external power supply is responsible for its proprietary ABG e+ screed heat system.

The electric paver can handle widths of up to 21.3 feet (6.5 meters), making it a good choice for most paving scenarios, especially in emission- and noise-sensitive job sites. We’ve all seen and heard a diesel asphalt paver in operation—I vividly remember when the street I grew up on got its first layer of asphalt decades ago, and while I was fascinated by all the machinery, I still remember that the site was extremely loud.

This, coupled with the fact that an electric paver emits up to 70% less carbon dioxide into the atmosphere, should make diesel construction equipment a thing of the past—at least in crowded, already polluted urban areas.

What’s more, because an electric powertrain has significantly fewer moving parts compared to a diesel lump, a lot of maintenance tasks simply disappear, along with their associated costs. At the same time, though, construction companies need to install charging equipment at their depots and find ways of topping up their rigs on the jobsite.

New York federal judge denies Novig’s injunction bid, rules its sports event contracts are not swaps

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The decision allows the state to require Novig either to obtain a state gambling license or to prohibit the listing and trading of sports event contracts on its platform.

A federal judge in New York denied a preliminary injunction sought by sports prediction market platform Novig, ruling that its sports-related event contracts do not qualify as swaps under the Commodity Exchange Act.

According to a Tuesday court filing, U.S. District Judge Colleen McMahon of the Southern District of New York concluded that the state may apply its gambling and sports-wagering laws to Novig’s activity. The decision allows New York to require Novig either to obtain a state gambling license or prohibit the listing and trading of sports event contracts on its platform.

Ludlow Exchange, LLC, the legal entity behind Novig, received the Commodity Futures Trading Commission's designation as a contract market in June 2026 and began offering sports event contracts to New York customers in August.

On Aug. 5, 2026, the company sued New York Attorney General Letitia James and officials at the New York State Gaming Commission, arguing that federal commodities law preempts state regulation of its products. It contended that the CFTC has "exclusive jurisdiction" over event contracts, which qualify as swaps under the act.

Judge McMahon rejected that argument at the preliminary injunction stage. She wrote that Novig’s sports event contracts “bear more than a passing resemblance to sports betting” and fall outside the CEA’s definition of a swap.

"The Court concludes that Novig’s contracts do not depend on events or contingencies that are ‘associated with a potential financial, economic, or commercial consequence’ within the meaning of § 1a(47)(A)(ii). Accordingly, they are not ‘swaps’ under the CEA," the filing said.

The ruling follows a similar decision earlier this year by another New York federal judge that denied Kalshi's request to block state authorities from enforcing gambling laws on its prediction market platform. Novig is also litigating the same issue in multiple other states, as Kalshi, Polymarket, and other prediction market platforms are doing.

"NY Attorney General Letitia James could literally cross the street and file a state-court civil enforcement action against Novig as soon as today," sports betting and gaming lawyer Daniel Wallach wrote on X.

Wallach added that he's seeing a larger shift where state regulators may move from defending their enforcement authority to exercising it proactively in state court. The lawyer said federal courts within the Second Circuit have now issued 10 rulings in sports prediction-market litigation, all of which have favored the states.

The Block has reached out to Novig for comment.

Taiwan Creative Content Fest Takeaways: ‘Avatar’ VFX House Brings Its AI Ambitions to Taipei as Character IP Booms

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Taiwan Creative Content Fest (TCCF) wrapped its 2026 edition in Taipei with a reshaped show floor, as animation and character IP companies moved to the front of the market.

They made up the largest exhibitor group, at 29 of 107 exhibitors, up from 19 in 2025, according to post-event figures from organizer the Taiwan Creative Content Agency (TAICCA). Of this year’s exhibitors, 69 were Taiwanese and 38 came from overseas, compared with 117 exhibitors in total last year. Related Stories MCU Surprise: ‘VisionQuest’ Finale Teases the Return of [SPOILER] in ‘Avengers: Doomsday’ 'Risa and the Wind Phone' Wins Audience Award at Miami Film Festival Gems - Film News in Brief

Elsewhere on the floor, television and media networks rose from 20 to 22 exhibitors, and new media, streaming and entertainment companies from six to eight. Film and TV production and distribution companies dropped from 22 to 16, digital tech, VFX and immersive media from 15 to eight, and publishing, comics and webtoons from 12 to five.

The Pitching program presented 93 projects and stories, including entries from TAICCA School, to industry participants from 28 countries. TAICCA is still compiling buyer attendance and the value of business done.

The headline deal came on opening day, Nov. 6, when Weta FX, the New Zealand visual effects company behind “The Lord of the Rings” and “Avatar,” signed a memorandum of understanding with TAICCA aimed at making Taiwan an AI hub for Asia’s content industries. Weta FX will partner with Taiwanese animation and VFX outfit Moonshine Studio and will set up a technology platform to back Taiwanese film and TV projects, along with exchanges and internships for young Taiwanese filmmakers.

Weta FX CEO Daniel Seah said studio legal departments had ruled out any use of generative AI on their projects early last year. By the turn of the year, clients were asking about Weta’s AI strategy, mostly to find out how much money the technology could save.

“It’s, ‘If you don’t have an AI strategy, don’t bother to work with us,’” Seah said of the period since the third quarter of this year.

Seah said AI had become a national strategy in Taiwan on the back of the island’s semiconductor industry, and Moonshine had been moving quickly as a result.

Japanese studios brought their own AI work to Taipei. Toei, the studio behind “Kamen Rider” and the Super Sentai series that became “Power Rangers,” has adopted rules for generative AI and is already using it for backgrounds, president Yoshimura Fumio said. “I believe AI is not something that takes away jobs, but a tool that expands the possibilities of expression and business,” he said.

At a TCCF panel on AI in animation, OLM Digital board director Yotsukura Tatsuo presented a coloring tool, developed under a Japanese government-backed research project, that learns a show’s look from three hand-painted frames. On the same panel, Charles Beirnaert, chief creative officer of French studio Blue Spirit, said the “Blue Eye Samurai” studio is barred from using AI on its Netflix work. Taiwanese technology company imCore Corp. launched Core Studio, an AI filmmaking platform with more than 24 specialized agents, at TCCF on Nov. 7.

“From a television broadcaster’s perspective, we’ve seen a significant increase in Vietnamese buyers this year,” said Grace Chan, a program planner in acquisitions and sales at Taiwan Television Enterprise (TTV). “Last year, Korean buyers and potential partners made up the largest group.”

Southeast Asia ran through the program. K7 Media’s Trang Nguyen presented a study of Indonesia, Southeast Asia’s biggest film market with box office revenue of $352.9 million in 2025, and pointed to horror as a natural meeting ground for Taiwanese and Indonesian producers. Thai director Thiti Srinuan explained how music video revenue bankrolled much of Taibaan Studio’s film universe before “The Undertaker” grossed more than THB700 million ($20.8 million) in Thailand. The top Pitching prize, the $35,000 TAICCA × CNC Feature Film Grand Award, went to “Somewhere in the South,” a Malaysian feature written and directed by Tan Ce Ding and produced by Anthony Chen, the Singaporean director of “Ilo Ilo.”

Japan was also looking at Taiwan. Cinematographer Wayne Lo, at TCCF for the first time, met several Japanese teams and found that up-and-coming Japanese filmmakers see the market and working directly with TAICCA as routes to getting projects made. “At the pitching sessions, I also sensed growing interest in Taiwanese content in Japan,” Lo said, though he noted that “Japan still offers a wider range of film genres than Taiwan.”

Toei’s Yoshimura said Japanese films were the most-released titles in Taiwan in 2025 and second only to U.S. films at the box office. He wants long-term tokusatsu partnerships and international co-productions with Taiwan.

The jump in animation and character IP exhibitors was echoed in the sessions.

Toei is launching a games label whose first three titles are originals rather than adaptations of its existing IP. “We aim to bring game creators’ talent on board over the long term as a new vein of IP creation, and make games the starting point for stories in place of novels and comics,” Yoshimura said.

Microdrama producers are thinking the same way. Sawamura Naomichi, founder of Japanese microdrama app Bump, said the company is considering adapting its microdramas into manga or animation.

Publishing, comics and webtoon exhibitors were down, but books and comics still drew interest through Pitching. Mirror Fiction’s “The Three Witches of Shetou” won both the TAICCA Award: Best Story and the friDay Video Original Story Award. Chang Chen-yu, producer at World Softest Production Film Co., said Taiwanese comic artist Gao Yan’s “The Song About Green” drew considerable attention after its TCCF presentation, and meeting requests were up on last year.

“Companies from Thailand, South Korea, Singapore and even Europe have expressed interest in collaborating,” Chang said. “This year, I’ve felt more strongly that creative talent and resources are concentrated in Asia, but the distinctiveness and audience appeal of the material remain key.”

TAICCA’s own audience research backs the push. Among viewers of Taiwanese film and TV, 40% had also watched Taiwanese screen adaptations, and the study flagged romance as a promising genre for cross-platform development, with links to comics, popular music and mobile games.

Telecoms Put Money Into Taiwanese Content

TAICCA presented the slates of its four joint-venture funds, which have backed 27 projects and drawn NT$4.9 billion in private capital alongside NT$740 million from the agency, about $180 million in all. Two of those ventures are partnerships with Taiwanese telecoms.

“Beyond OTT [streaming] platforms, the sharp decline in advertising has been the most visible challenge for television stations. Acquisition budgets, meanwhile, have not come down,” TTV’s Chan said. “Through the connections made at the market, we hope to find content that suits our needs and explore original productions and co-productions to diversify risk.”

The Chunghwa Digital Cultural and Creative Fund, majority owned by Chunghwa Telecom, totals NT$1 billion ($32 million) and backs both projects and companies. Enjoy Entertainment, TAICCA’s partnership with telecom Far EasTone, invested in 15 projects in its first 10 months. Its slate also includes a Taiwanese remake of Korean series “Our Blues,” which launches globally on Netflix on Dec. 3. The momomini Kids Content Fund focuses on children’s content, which COO Phoebe Tsai said had not previously been treated as a distinct investment category in Taiwan.

One of Enjoy’s investments ties the week together. “The Rope Curse 4: Kuntilanak,” a Taiwan-Indonesia horror collaboration that Toei also invested in, took NT$80 million ($2.6 million) at the Taiwanese box office, a franchise record.

Manchester United have agreed a deal in principle for Mauricio Pochettino to become their new manager, sources have told Football Insider.

A verbal agreement is already in place with the Argentine, who is set to leave his job in charge of the USA national team, following a breakthrough in talks on Tuesday.

Sir Jim Ratcliffe is set to rubber-stamp Michael Carrick's sacking on Wednesday, with Pochettino already in line to take the reins.

Sources say that the former Tottenham boss will move to Old Trafford alongside his long-standing assistants Jesus Perez and Miki D'Agostino, as well as goalkeeping coach Toni Jimenez.

United are set to pay Pochettino's £7.44million buy-out clause, with the club adamant that the fee is "not a problem" for them. They will also have to pay for his coaching staff to leave the USA team.

A decision regarding Carrick's future has been imminent in recent days after executives Omar Berrada and Jason Wilcox decided on Monday to recommend to the club's ownership that he should be dismissed.

United have been linked with moves for several bosses in recent weeks, but Pochettino has come from outsider status to win the race.

Eddie Howe and Julian Nagelsmann have both been extensively linked with the role, while Aston Villa's Unai Emery was also a target at one stage.

However, it is Pochettino who has now come to the fore and will step into the home dugout at Old Trafford.

The 54-year-old is a long-term target for the United hierarchy, and they have finally been able to convince him to join.

Pochettino has never hidden his desire to return to the Premier League, and he will now get the chance to lead United back to glory.

Former Tottenham scout Bryan King told Football Insider earlier this week that United's players have "wanted Carrick out" for several weeks now.

The ex-recruitment man believes that there was a clear "breakdown" in communication between the 45-year-old and his group.

King outlined that the majority of the squad had completely lost faith in Carrick, and have not been playing for him in recent games.

The writing has been on the wall for the former midfielder for several weeks, and his exit is now set to be sealed.

Since the start of Q4, the crypto market has continued to weaken amid volatility. Major asset prices have pulled back from their highs, and market participants have become more cautious. As the market cools, HTX has focused its efforts on solidifying its trading fundamentals and offering diverse yield generation pathways. In October, HTX saw a significant overall improvement in the market depth of its core spot trading pairs, with the 0.05% depth for the BTC/USDT pair increasing by 78%. At the end of October, HTX Earn launched the APY Boost Promotion as part of the HTX Winter Campaign, delivering stable yield options for users amid fluctuating market conditions.

Overall Spot Depth Enhancement, Ensuring Smoother Execution for Large-Volume Trades

In October, HTX continued to strengthen its collaboration with professional market makers, improving market-making support, order book depth, and the overall trading environment, which contributed to an overall improvement in the market depth of its core spot trading pairs. Taking BTC/USDT as an example, the pair’s 0.01% depth increased by 42% in October, while its 0.05% depth jumped by 78%. Deeper market depth ensures that orders of the same size have a smaller price impact, allowing large-volume trades to be executed closer to market price. For core assets like BTC, this means users can easily execute trades even during periods of severe market volatility.

Centered around the two core assets of BTC and ETH, HTX also rolled out a series of trading rewards in October, including trading challenges, fee rebates, trading lucky draws, and iPhone Duo theme activities, with the flagship trading challenge attracting over 10,000 participants.

Analysis: How Netanyahu Lost His Bet on Trump

While Israelis were still coming to terms with the uncertain outcome of their own Oct. 27 elections, U.S. President Donald Trump chose the day of the U.S. midterms to further discombobulate the Israeli public. On Fox News, he announced not only that he was ending U.S. military assistance to Israel by the end of the year but also that Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu had agreed that the assistance should end. In an interview with Fox & Friends, Trump remarked: “As far as paying Israel, Bibi Netanyahu and I … made a deal months ago that we’re stopping, and they’re fine with it.”

It is true that Netanyahu told the Economist in January that he hoped to “taper off” Israeli dependence on U.S. military aid over the next decade. But he has given no indication of any agreement for military assistance to terminate as soon as this year. Feeling the pressure, Netanyahu’s office is now spinning the end of U.S. military assistance as a “transition out from aid towards partnership and mutual investment."

While Israelis were still coming to terms with the uncertain outcome of their own Oct. 27 elections, U.S. President Donald Trump chose the day of the U.S. midterms to further discombobulate the Israeli public. On Fox News, he announced not only that he was ending U.S. military assistance to Israel by the end of the year but also that Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu had agreed that the assistance should end. In an interview with Fox & Friends, Trump remarked: “As far as paying Israel, Bibi Netanyahu and I … made a deal months ago that we’re stopping, and they’re fine with it.”

It is true that Netanyahu told the Economist in January that he hoped to “taper off” Israeli dependence on U.S. military aid over the next decade. But he has given no indication of any agreement for military assistance to terminate as soon as this year. Feeling the pressure, Netanyahu’s office is now spinning the end of U.S. military assistance as a “transition out from aid towards partnership and mutual investment.”

The backlash in Israel was immediate. Trump’s announcement raises critical questions regarding what political price was negotiated—and specifically, whether any agreement to end U.S. aid served as the quid pro quo for Trump’s endorsement of Netanyahu in Israel’s election.

Former Prime Minister Ehud Barak accused Netanyahu of concocting “a stinking deal” that traded U.S. military assistance for a presidential endorsement, concluding that Netanyahu had “exceeded his authority” and was “utterly unfit to lead a country.” Opposition leader Gadi Eisenkot, who may yet replace Netanyahu as prime minister, was less conspiratorial but equally scathing. “Israel’s special relationship with the United States is a central component of its national security,” he wrote on X. “The fact that the security establishment is learning, together with Israeli citizens, about a change in the strategic relationship through the media shows just how much Benjamin Netanyahu’s conduct is a real danger.”

That Trump chose to make this announcement on Election Day speaks volumes about the collapse of Israel’s standing across the U.S. political spectrum. He evidently believed that it would resonate with voters amid rising public anger over inflation and the cost of living. The question of why a high-tech powerhouse requires billions in taxpayer-funded military aid carries growing political weight, even if much of the aid is reinvested in the U.S. defense industry.

The real elephant in the room, however, is the ongoing war with Iran and anger over U.S. support for Israel. Prominent Republicans, including Vice President J.D. Vance, have openly identified the conflict as the principal catalyst for the party’s electoral thrashing. Writing on X in the immediate aftermath of the vote, Vance broke ranks with traditional White House messaging, declaring bluntly: “The reason we lost is the war. … [E]veryone knows why we lost. And any honest conversation about where we go from here must start there.” He also claimed that voters wanted to see a change in the relationship with Israel. Vance highlighted internal polling showing that 2024 Trump voters who defected to the Democrats in the midterms specifically cited an end to the war, economic relief from soaring fuel prices, and a fundamental shift in Washington’s relationship with Israel as their primary demands. In an interview with CBS’s 60 Minutes on Sunday, Trump did not disagree with Vance’s observation that the Iran war caused the Republican defeat in the midterms, adding that “there is a great dislike, incredibly, for Israel.”

While it was Trump who launched a war against Iran in February, Netanyahu never concealed his desire for a joint campaign. Even following Operation Epic Fury, he has consistently tried to persuade Trump to renew the war, treating the Iranian threat not merely as an existential regional issue but as a political prop to distract the Israeli electorate from his leadership failures in the lead-up to and aftermath of Oct. 7, 2023.

Ahead of the midterms, the New York Times published a report claiming that Israel had shared intelligence with the United States and Gulf countries about possible Iranian plans to use chemical weapons against them. U.S. officials met the intelligence with skepticism, believing it was part of a coordinated effort by Netanyahu to push Trump toward renewed strikes.

Netanyahu’s rhetoric on Iran is riddled with glaring contradictions. Having publicly trumpeted historic success in dismantling Iran’s nuclear capabilities in June 2025, he has reversed course to manufacture an immediate, existential urge for military confrontation. On Oct. 25, Netanyahu posted an AI-generated image depicting a nuclear blast over Tel Aviv, warning voters that rejecting his leadership invited a “second Holocaust.” Political opponents accused him of manufacturing a crisis with Iran as an attempt to delay the election. This triggered an unprecedented rift with Israel Defense Forces Chief of Staff Eyal Zamir, who resisted political orders that risked sparking an immediate, unwarranted regional conflagration. Yair Golan of the Democrats declared that Netanyahu lacks “the necessary public trust” and “moral legitimacy or even basic capability” to lead such a campaign, while former Israeli Ambassador to Germany Jeremy Issacharoff noted that Netanyahu’s political machinations over Iran “severely damage Israel’s credibility abroad.”

During the opening session of the new Knesset on Tuesday, Netanyahu insisted that while his government had removed the immediate existential threat, Iran was renewing its nuclear program and had not given up on its intent to destroy Israel. He declared that the only way to remove this existential threat was by toppling the regime, trumpeting for good measure that his government had “changed the face of the Middle East.”

Netanyahu’s endgame is clearly at odds with the objectives of the Trump administration, which views military force primarily as leverage to reopen the Strait of Hormuz and force Tehran to climb down over its nuclear program. In his incendiary speech, Netanyahu also asserted that only a national unity government—presumably under his leadership—would enable Israel to meet the upcoming challenges, while accusing Israel’s Arab lawmakers of seeking the nation’s destruction. Eisenkot countered immediately, stating that Netanyahu’s “hate-filled” speech demonstrated that he was unfit for office. He has clearly failed to internalize the fact that, even among his erstwhile allies in the Republican party, his political standing is becoming increasingly toxic.

Netanyahu retains power for now as a caretaker prime minister while Eisenkot seeks to form a new government. Renewed hostilities with Iran in coordination with the United States would have been highly convenient for Netanyahu, as this could have delayed the opening of the newly elected parliament and formation of a new government. His overarching goal remains to block an opposition coalition that includes or is supported by Arab parties, who emerged as pivotal kingmakers in this election.

Deploying the Trump playbook to contest the election results, Netanyahu’s loyalist Shin Bet security chief, David Zini, has targeted Arab opposition lawmakers with unfounded investigations, raising grave constitutional alarm. Even though Eisenkot has received the mandate to form a government, his narrow victory and the opposition’s lingering hesitation toward joining forces with the Arab parties make coalition-building highly uncertain.

While right-wing factions in Israel view Netanyahu’s relationship with Trump and a renewed U.S.-Israel campaign against Iran as a strategic win, the long-term fallout could be severe. In putting all his eggs into the Republican basket, Netanyahu has wrecked U.S. bipartisan support for Israel. Following a decisive Democratic shift in Congress, he finds himself dangerously exposed: He faces fierce critics on the American left and an angry Republican base looking for a political scapegoat.

Trump is now signaling that a renewal of war is “highly unlikely” if talks with Iran show progress. Yet after last week’s midterm collapse, his close ally Chris Ruddy noted on the BBC, there was an 80 percent chance that the U.S. president could still authorize military action against Tehran. Netanyahu thus retains every incentive to gamble on regional escalation in order to entrench his political survival, even at the direct expense of Israel’s democratic resilience and its international standing. It appears that he has nowhere left to run.

An unpredictable lame-duck U.S. president may feel that he has little left to lose, but for Netanyahu and an increasingly isolated Israeli public, the stakes could not be higher.

Azriel Bermant is an associate researcher at the Institute of International Relations Prague as well as the author of Margaret Thatcher and the Middle East and the Azriel’s Substack newsletter.

I've tested the Apple Watch 12, Galaxy Watch 9 and Pixel Watch 5 — here is the smartwatch I'd buy with my own money

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Apple vs Google vs Samsung in a smartwatch showdown

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

I’ve spent the past few months diving deep into the new Apple Watch Series 12, Google Pixel Watch 5, and Samsung Galaxy Watch 9. In fact, I wrote long-form reviews of the latter two and have tested the former head-to-head against the competition over a dozen times.

Hi, I’m Dan, and I lead our smartwatch coverage here at Tom’s Guide, and I often get asked, “OS preference aside, which smartwatch from the BIG THREE would I actually buy in 2026?”

It’s a difficult question to answer, given that all three models have advantages over their competitors. Moreover, Apple, Google, and Samsung all make seriously lovely wearables — some of the best smartwatches in 2026 — brimming with smart, health, safety, and connectivity features.

But enough excuses. Below, I’ve compared the Apple Watch 12, Pixel Watch 5, and Galaxy Watch 9 across six categories — cost, design, display, fitness/recovery features, health/safety features, and battery — with a winner or winners declared for each. If you’re short on time, you can find my ultimate verdict at the very end.

The Apple Watch Series 12 (42mm) and Pixel Watch 5 (41mm) each start at $399 for the smaller respective models without cellular. (Note: The Pixel Watch 5 is currently $100 off at Amazon.) The Galaxy Watch 9 (40mm) is a tiny bit cheaper, starting at $379.

If you spring for the larger size with cellular connectivity, the Apple Watch Series 12 (46mm) is the priciest at $549, followed by the Pixel Watch 5 (45mm) at $529, with the Samsung Galaxy Watch 9 (44mm) being the best bargain of the fully-loaded big boys at $459.

Based on value alone, Samsung wins this category; however, a savings of $20 to $100 is unlikely to be the determining factor in which smartwatch I’d ultimately buy. Moreover, I prefer smaller watches and don't really need cellular, diminishing Samsung’s pricing advantage.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

The Apple Watch Series 12, Google Pixel Watch 5, and Samsung Galaxy Watch 9 are all extremely well-built, durable, and comfortable devices based on my extended testing. Each boasts 50 meters of water resistance, enough for surface-level swims, with metal cases and scratch-resistant displays.

No matter which model you go with, you get two physical controls along the side of the case. On the Galaxy Watch 9, those controls come in the form of two identical-looking buttons. On the Pixel Watch 5 and Apple Watch 12, you get a physical button and a digital crown that can be both turned and pressed, making it more valuable than a standard button.

As a result, I prefer the controls of the latest Apple Watch and Pixel Watch to those of the newest Galaxy Watch.

Each of the major manufacturers has settled on a unique case shape. Apple is a square, Google is a circle, and Samsung is a squarish circle.

In 2026, each of the major full-featured smartwatch manufacturers has settled on a unique case shape. Apple is a square, Google is a circle, and Samsung is a squarish circle. Of the three shapes, I prefer Apple’s the best.

The Galaxy Watch 9’s circular display mounted on a rounded square case results in what feels like wasted space around the bezel. Meanwhile, the Pixel Watch 5’s circular case coupled with its domed Actua 360 circular display makes the watch feel a little too bubbly/sit rather high on my wrist.

With the best controls and sleekest case design, Apple gets my nod in this category.

All three of these smartwatches sport bright, crisp, and well-saturated AMOLED screens. However, Samsung and Google’s displays offer a higher maximum output (they get brighter) than Apple’s, making them a bit easier to read in direct sunlight.

The latest Apple Watch Series 12 screen maxes out at 2,000 nits compared to 3,000 nits for the Galaxy Watch 9 and Pixel Watch 5.

Winner: Google Pixel Watch 5 and Samsung Galaxy Watch 9

I’ve spent a ton of time exploring the vast array of fitness tracking and workout planning features found in the best smartwatch models of 2026. That said, for basic insights into your physical activity, sleep quality, and overall well-being, the Apple Watch 12, Pixel Watch 5, or Samsung Galaxy Watch 9 will all serve you well.

However, for more advanced workout training and recovery insights that don’t require a subscription, Samsung has both Apple and Google beat. Don’t get me wrong, the new Google Health app (formerly the Fitbit app) is jam-packed with features comparable to what the Samsung Health app offers. However, the best ones require a monthly Google Health Premium subscription ($9.99 a month or $99 for the year). That’s not the case for the Galaxy Watch.

Samsung’s free daily sleep reports are also the most comprehensive of the three, [and] only the Galaxy Watch 9 and Apple Watch 12 offer FDA-cleared sleep apnea detection.

Samsung’s free daily sleep reports are also the most comprehensive of the three, in my opinion. Also, only the Galaxy Watch 9 and Apple Watch 12 offer FDA-cleared sleep apnea detection.

Apple’s health and fitness tracking chops are decent, but I still feel the Cupertino Kid is catching up to its two biggest competitors when it comes to dynamic wellness insights. For example, Apple just got a daily readiness score this year. Samsung, meanwhile, has offered an “Energy Score” since 2024, right around the same time Google launched its daily readiness score.

Apple’s sleep score feature is also relatively new (launched in 2025), and from my experience, it tends to be the most optimistic of the three, which is either a good thing or a bad thing, depending on how you look at it.

All three smartwatches have some form of fall/crash detection, which can automatically call for help when triggered, as well as user-activated SOS safety tools. You also get alerts for high/low heart rates, along with AFib detection and SpO2 readings.

However, only the Apple Watch 12 offers 24-7 hypertension alerts, designed to detect signs of high blood pressure early. On the other hand, only the Pixel Watch 5 offers Loss of Pulse Detection and Breathing Emergency Detection, both of which work similarly to fall/crash detection.

The Pixel Watch 5 also supports new Blood Pressure Trends and Insulin Resistance Trends, along with Breathing Quality Trends, making it arguably the king of on-wrist health and safety in 2026.

On paper, the Apple Watch 12 is supposed to last up to 24 hours per charge, while the Galaxy Watch 9 and Pixel Watch 5 are rated at up to 40 hours per charge. In reality, both the Apple Watch and Galaxy Watch easily meet their battery life ratings.

The Pixel Watch 5, meanwhile, far surpasses the 40-hour rating in my testing. I reviewed the larger model and averaged closer to 48 hours per charge.

Ultimately, if I were spending my hard-earned money on one of the top smartwatches in 2026 — ignoring smartphone OS preferences — I’d likely choose the Apple Watch Series 12 over the Google Pixel Watch 5 or Samsung Galaxy Watch 9.

The main reason is that I prefer the design of the Apple Watch Series 12 to the competition. I also genuinely appreciate Apple’s simple approach to daily fitness and health tracking — via “closing your rings” — even if it's a little bit basic for hardcore users.

I prefer the design of the Apple Watch Series 12 to the competition. I also genuinely appreciate Apple’s simple approach to daily fitness and health tracking.

Apple is still playing catch-up a bit when it comes to health and advanced training metrics, but the brand has definitely made up for lost ground in 2026 with the debut of a daily readiness score, heart rate variability tracking, the forthcoming health age metric, and a greater overall emphasis on tracking long-term wellness trends.

Still, Google has Apple beat when it comes to on-wrist safety, and Samsung’s health/fitness tools remain a bit more comprehensive than Apple’s... for now. All that said, I really wish Apple would step up its game when it comes to battery performance.

Which of these three high-end 2026 smartwatches would you choose? Let me know below.

Zelda Switch 2 Pro controller restock: Live restock updates at Amazon, Target, and Best Buy

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

We have been tracking live inventory updates for the new Zelda Switch 2 console, but the matching special-edition Pro Controller gamepad has become just as elusive. Instead of sitting on store shelves, the standalone controller is vanishing within seconds of going live online, turning into one of the most frustrating gear hunts of the entire holiday season.

With preorders completely cleared out and launch day behind us, landing one now means playing the restock waiting game. Digital storefronts might look completely barren, but stock is still quietly trickling through distribution pipelines in surprise, unannounced micro-waves.

If you want to get your hands on it the next time it is in stock, here's what's going on across different major retailers.

Before you start jumping between tabs, bookmark these retailer pages to catch surprise drops:

Best Buy — Online drops occur in waves via a digital waiting line, with scattered walk-in stock available at select physical stores.

GameStop — Web orders are off, so your best bet is to walk in and check directly with your neighborhood store.

Nintendo Store — Sold out online. You can add it to your wishlist to get notified if Nintendo opens another batch.

Target — Sold out online, but early-morning in-store pickup options occasionally go live depending on local truck deliveries.

You will notice Walmart is missing from our quick-buy links above. While the controller has a product listing on the site, stock sold directly by Walmart for the official $99.99 list price is completely gone.

Every buy button currently active is run by third-party marketplace resellers charging inflated, price-gouging sums. Do not pay those prices. If you search Walmart on your own, always filter the seller option to "Walmart" so you only see genuine retail inventory.

The feeding frenzy is directly tied to the arrival of The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time on Nintendo Switch 2. The ground-up remake reworks the 1998 classic with upgraded visuals, modern camera controls, rearranged dungeon puzzles, and a full orchestral score. Long play sessions in sprawling dungeons feel more natural on a traditional, ergonomic gamepad than on standard Joy-Con grips, making this matching controller the first thing players want on their desks.

Supply is also tight across the board. In an interview about launch shortages, Bill van Zyll, Vice President and General Manager of Nintendo Latin America, admitted that the company had underestimated worldwide demand for the Zelda 40th-anniversary collection. While he confirmed that replenishment plans are underway for the hardware, software, and special controller, Nintendo has not committed to specific restock dates or shipment quantities. The best tech deal this week The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

Until the production lines catch up, expect store inventory to pop up in quick, random bursts rather than steady, predictable drops.

The special-edition Pro Controller gamepad features decorative illustrations and subtle gold patterns inspired by the wider Zelda universe. It carries every modern upgrade found on the standard generation-two pad. You get built-in motion sensors for precision bow aiming, HD Rumble 2 for detailed vibration feedback, an integrated amiibo scanner, and a capture button for grabbing screenshots on the fly.

Nintendo also added a dedicated C button to open the Switch 2 GameChat hub, remappable GL and GR shortcut buttons on the back grips, a standard 3.5mm headphone jack, and up to 40 hours of battery life on a single charge. Best of all, it works across the entire Switch family, including the original Nintendo Switch, Switch OLED, Switch Lite, and the new Nintendo Switch 2.

Landing a surprise drop comes down to speed and a little bit of prep:

Dodge third-party marketplace sellers: Sites like Walmart and Amazon let outside merchants charge whatever they want. Always check the "Sold and shipped by" label to ensure you are buying from the actual retailer at the $99.99 list price.

Save your checkout information beforehand: Taking the time to type in a security code or shipping address during a drop will cost you your cart. Keep your profiles logged in with default payment methods saved.

Do not refresh Best Buy's waiting line: When Best Buy runs a drop, it puts shoppers into an automated queue. Hitting refresh resets your spot and sends you to the very back of the line.

Check physical stores in the morning: Online checkout bots can wipe out digital inventory in seconds. Visiting Target, Best Buy, or GameStop right when doors open often turns up fresh shipment boxes that never made it onto website listings.

Retailers love using limited-edition hardware and themed accessories as digital bait during Black Friday and Cyber Monday. It is very likely that stores are sitting on a handful of units reserved specifically for holiday sales events.

The catch is that Black Friday restocks are pure chaos. You are up against overloaded checkout servers, holiday gift shoppers, and scalper scripts all hitting the site at once. If an "Add to Cart" button flickers live on Amazon or Best Buy before the holiday rush officially starts, take the shot immediately so you don't have to fight the crowds later.

Finding one right now is definitely an endurance test, but holding out for that $99.99 list price beats paying double to a reseller every time.

Keeping hold of Company of Heroes was essential for Relic to leave Sega, Relic CEO says – "Without it we would have been a dead studio"

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In the nearly 30 years since they opened their doors, Relic have been an enormously influential studio. Responsible for lauded RTS series Homeworld, Dawn of War, and Company of Heroes, they also dabbled in other genres with third-person shooter The Outfit and the original Warhammer 40,000: Space Marine. Yet, despite this history, in 2024, when Relic were negotiating their sale from Sega to UK investment firm Emona Capita, CEO Justin Dowdeswell knew the rights to Company of Heroes had to be part of the deal. "Without it we would have been a dead studio," he tells me.

Without naming names Dowdeswell points out that there are "numerous examples" of studios that weren't able to survive on their name alone. Not only did Relic need the ability to make more games in the series that they were known for, but they also needed the income from back catalogue sales a series like Company of Heroes provides.

Dowdeswell joined Relic shortly after Sega acquired the studio in 2013. This was in the immediate aftermath of publisher THQ's bankruptcy auction, where studios and franchises were sold off to the highest bidder. While Sega bought the studio, they didn't buy the licenses to everything Relic had created. "The other games that Relic made – Homeworld, Impossible Creatures, The Outfit... formative games in many people's memory – they were all split up, scattered," Dowdeswell says. "I think that was... I won't say a tragedy, but it's unfortunate because Relic is the home of those games. It would have been great for them to stay with the studio."

Just as a hint of the messy history of acquisitions and auctions: this year we see Dawn of War 4 coming from studio King Art Games and published by Deep Silver. Deep Silver are a subdivision of Plaion, formerly called Koch Media, which was acquired by Embracer Group back when they were called THQ Nordic. Not that they were actually the THQ that originally published Relic's Dawn of War, Nordic Games renamed themselves THQ Nordic after they bought the THQ trademark at auction.

As for Homeworld, Borderlands developer Gearbox picked up the license to the sci-fi RTS in the bankruptcy auction, then contracted Blackbird Interactive's support on the remasters, before handing the latter lead development on Homeworld 3. That chain of events does have a more clear throughline to Relic's past however, as Blackbird was created by two of Relic's co-founders Rob Cunningham and Jon Aaron Kambeitz. (Speaking of studios struggling without the series they made their name with, Blackbird was on the verge of bankruptcy before Gearbox got in touch with the Homeworld work. So we very nearly existed in a world where they never got to make the brilliant Homeworld: Deserts of Kharak and Hardspace Shipbreaker.)

None of this is intended to be a knock on Dawn of War 4. From what I've played it's a very good Warhammer 40K RTS, if it perhaps sticks a little closely to the model laid down by Relic's original. What do I know, though, talking about their own games, Dowdeswell tells me that while "one of the messages to the studio is we can't just make [the same] things that we made 20 years ago and hope it goes well", re-releasing the 20-year-old Dawn of War sold very well for Relic and netted the team some very positive reviews on Steam. "There's a bit of a paradox there," he adds"

Anyway, this is all getting side-tracked. Back to holding onto Company of Heroes when separating from Sega. "Fighting for Company of Heroes when leaving Sega was crucial because I already lamented the fact that the other games didn't come with Relic," Dowdeswell says. "I couldn't really imagine a Relic where none of the games that the studio has made and was known for were any longer part of it."

Relic and new owner UK investment firm Emona Capital LLP were able to negotiate Company of Heroes as part of the sale from Sega and since then the studio has spent the past two years focusing largely on supporting the WW2 RTS and restoring their back catalogue. The most recent update is the massive 3.0 patch to mark the series' 20th anniversary. Which, among many changes, overhauls the Mediterranean-set RTS game's special effects, audio systems, NPC AI, and makes significant changes to the singleplayer grand strategy-inspired Italy campaign, as well as adding a whole set of autumn and winter-themed assets for community map makers. They've also released a series of DLCs adding new factions to Company of Heroes 3's skirmishes and multiplayer, re-released Dawn of War with a definitive edition and announced they're doing the same for the original Company of Heroes, along with Blackbird Interactive's help. Dowdeswell says foundation building masks a lot of the work going on behind the scenes: "We now self-publish all of this," he explains. "There were a number of things that we had to learn, skills we had to develop, capacity and capability that we had to build at the studio very, very quickly."

I ask if it was always the plan to focus on their back catalogue, or if they had plans to release new games. Dowdeswell says spending four years quietly working on a new game wasn't an option: "It's almost impossible to do it that way because of the current climate in the industry. We don't have a financial safety net." Though, the team do have larger ambitions. "We want to make new games, we want to provide a modern Relic game," Dowdeswell says. "That ambition to re-establish Relic as an innovator in this strategy space is a big part of what drives our creative direction on new games." Focusing on updating Company of Heroes 3, releasing DLC for the game, and making definitive editions of Dawn of War and the original Company of Heroes is what gets the studio to the place it can make a new game.

However, Dowdeswell resists any framing of Relic's work on Company of Heroes 3 as spinning their wheels or resting on their laurels while working toward a new game. He argues the game "sits in between" Relic's past and its future: "It's an established game that came out three years ago, but it's also a way for us to take some chances, experiment a little bit, and while doing that staying really closely connected to the players, That's what the idea was behind the updates because it was a way to have very quick iterations and touch points with players and provide, honestly, a way for us to fund everything in the future."

As to what Relic is making next, Dowdeswell wouldn't say. Though, he revealed trying genres besides real-time strategy was "almost a certainty", before adding "I don't think it's going to be a vast departure for where we are, so it will still be strategic gameplay. We're not going to go build a racer."

It's Official, Zelda: Ocarina Of Time Has Sold Over 5 Million Copies In 4 Days

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nintendo has announced that The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time has sold a total of 5.2 million units in just four days.

This number includes 810,000 copies sold in Japan alone, and makes Ocarina of Time the fastest-selling Nintendo-published Switch 2 game since the console's launch. For added context, the N64 original sold an overall total of 7.6 million units, according to Video Game Sales wiki.

This also makes it the second highest-selling Switch 2 game, with initial sales putting it above Donkey Kong Bananza's lifetime sales at 5.05 million (as of 30th September 2026). The best-selling Switch 2 game remains Mario Kart World at 16.48 million.

It's not clear what the digital/physical split is at the time of writing, though we should have a slightly better picture of this once Famitsu releases the Japanese boxed charts later this week.

Nintendo Co., Ltd. (HQ: Kyoto, Minami-ku, Japan; President and Representative Director: Shuntaro Furukawa, “Nintendo” hereafter) announced that The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time for Nintendo Switch 2 sold over 5.2 million units worldwide (including 810,000 units sold in Japan) in the first four days following its launch on November 5, 2026.

This is the highest global sales for any Nintendo-published Nintendo Switch 2 game in its first four days.

The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time for Nintendo Switch 2, released worldwide on November 5, is a full remake of The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time, originally released for Nintendo 64 in 1998. Link travels between two time periods, as Young Link and Adult Link, making use of different abilities and items available in each period to progress through his adventure. Developed for Nintendo Switch 2, the game features reimagined visuals and enhanced playability, while preserving the experience of the original game.

The game also supports ZELDA NOTES, a service available within the Nintendo Switch App for compatible smart devices. By using ZELDA NOTES, players can access a variety of features that support gameplay.

In the UK, it was confirmed that Ocarina of Time was the second biggest physical launch in series history, coming in just behind Tears of the Kingdom. It stormed to the top of the charts to become the biggest physical video game launch of 2026 (so far – hi, GTA).

As for our review, we awarded Ocarina of Time a 9/10, stating it to be "the best way to play one of the most seminal games of all time":

"Ocarina of Time on Switch 2 is a stellar reimagining of the original, one that completely revamps the experience with stunning visuals, modernised gameplay, and vastly improved audio. Despite some minor blemishes, this is comfortably the most visually stunning work Nintendo has ever put out, and it’s unquestionably the best way to play one of the most seminal games of all time."

STAT+: MDCalc launches a ‘rumble strip’ for doctors using generative AI tools

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Test your knowledge with our new weekday mini crossword

Brittany Trang, Ph.D., covers AI in health and medicine: Does it actually work? Who benefits, or might be harmed? She writes the weekly AI Prognosis newsletter. Follow her on Threads, Mastodon, and Bluesky. You can reach Brittany on Signal at btrang.01.

MDCalc, a medical reference tool site, Wednesday announced the launch of CrossCover, which the company said is meant to help clinicians trust the answers of medical artificial intelligence chatbots while preserving patient and provider privacy.

Graham Walker, head of AI at MDCalc, said the company sees the tool as an accelerator for clinical generative AI. “We can take away some of the challenges and risks and problems with the technology so that physicians can feel more confident in using it,” he said.

CrossCover takes an approach that contradicts that of medical AI tools from OpenEvidence, Abridge, and electronic health record company Epic, which are trying to incorporate an ever-widening repertoire of patient, provider, and payer information into their recommendations. Rather than requiring clinicians to register with their national identification numbers or trying to mitigate risk by brokering privacy agreements that allow clinicians to enter protected patient health information, MDCalc’s tool focuses on stripping out those types of information from clinicians’ queries.

In an age of mistrust, communicating more isn’t enough. Influence comes from building networks

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Serbian President Aleksandar Vučić’s loss in recent elections was startling for a number of reasons. First, during his 14-year reign, Vučić had come to rule the country with an iron hand. Second was the scale: The opposition Student List candidates won 52.65% of the vote, while Vučić’s United Serbia party took only 39.2%.

But even more important, which you couldn’t see from all the big crowds and headlines, is what my friend the Serbian activist Srdja Popović calls the student’s “analog strategy.” Named Student in Every Village, it eschewed high-tech targeting databases and AI-generated graphics for old-fashioned door-to-door campaigning. 

This is part of a larger phenomenon that leaders need to recognize and act upon. As trust in institutions has diminished, traditional channels, such as carefully crafted press releases, slick promotions, and announcements from behind a podium are increasingly ineffective. Today, organizations need to build more visceral, on-the-ground networks to gain traction.  

The “Leaderless Revolutions”

In the early 2000s and 2010s, a series of popular revolutions brought down powerful authoritarian regimes. First came the color revolutions, which overthrew the remnants of communist dictatorships in Serbia, Georgia, and Ukraine. Then the uprisings of the Arab Spring sprang up across the Middle East, bringing similar pressure on hard-line despots.

Many referred to these as “leaderless revolutions” because they had no charismatic figurehead (like a Gandhi, Martin Luther King Jr., or Mandela). It seemed as if the streets just spontaneously filled with self-organizing networks of protesters, empowered by technology, to demand change. The brittle 20th-century-style regimes hardly knew what hit them. 

In 2013, political scientist Moisés Naím published The End of Power, in which he argued that because of increasing mobility and technological change, along with declining poverty, the power of institutions was diminishing. Power hadn’t ended exactly but, as he put it, power was becoming “easier to gain but harder to use or keep.”

While there was some truth to all of this, much of it was a mirage. I was there, in Kyiv, Ukraine, during the Orange Revolution in 2004, and it was clear even then that the protests were designed and planned by well-trained activists. Yes, technology played a part. Online message boards and text messaging were critical communication tools, but none of it just happened on its own. 

As Srdja recently put it to me, “There are two kinds of movements: spontaneous and successful.” Something else was going on.  

Saddleback’s Secret

When Rick Warren first arrived in Orange County, California, in 1979, he saw the opportunity to build a new kind of church. He had spent three months going door-to-door and found that while many residents identified themselves as Christians, they found church services boring and irrelevant. So he began to tailor his services and programs to meet their needs.

Today, his Saddleback Church is one of the largest congregations in the world, with 20,000 people attending services every week. Yet looks can be deceiving. What makes Warren such a powerful force isn’t those massive weekend events, but the thousands of small prayer groups that meet during the week.

The heart of the Saddleback movement is the groups of six to eight people who meet each week, build strong ties, and support each other in matters of faith, family, and career. It is the strong bonds, combined with the loose connections between those small groups, that give Saddleback its combination of massive reach and internal coherence.

My experiences in the Orange Revolution were similar. Yes, there were enormous crowds, but we largely operated in small groups, sometimes coordinating with other small groups, going online and texting to give and receive information. Later, as I got to know and work more closely with Srdja, I learned that these movements were designed that way—in no way leaderless, but very much decentralized. 

As I wrote in Cascades, transformational change is driven by small groups, loosely connected, but united by a common purpose. Decentralization is not the absence of leadership. It is a different form of leadership. The job of a leader is no longer to plan and organize tasks but to help those groups connect and inspire them with a sense of shared values and shared mission.  

The Disinformation Wars

When the COVID-19 pandemic hit in 2020, Dr. Anthony Fauci became a household name. Signaling institutional authority, he stood behind a podium, wore a white lab coat, and talked passionately about the “science” of the disease and its spread. He asked people to change their behaviors, wear masks, and distance themselves from their neighbors.

Yet many didn’t trust him. He felt distant, and they were skeptical that someone who felt so remote from their lives could have their best interests at heart. It was as if Fauci were a minister at the pulpit, but congregants never made it to the service. They were still in their prayer groups, reading from an entirely different hymn book, written by other people they saw in white lab coats just like Fauci’s on social media and YouTube but saying very different things. 

Recently, a team of researchers at the Massachusetts Institute of Technology looked into how we share information—and misinformation—in our little “prayer groups.” What they found was troubling. Their model suggests that when we’re surrounded by people who think like us, we share information more freely because we don’t expect to be challenged. We’re also less likely to check our facts, because we know that those we’re sharing with are less likely to inspect the facts themselves.

In Naím’s 2023 follow-up to his earlier book, The Revenge of Power, he showed how many of those same players that had been disrupted in earlier revolutions, like autocrats, corporations, and criminal networks, adapted the same tools that the movements used, such as the internet and social media, to discredit their opponents, reconstitute their power, and disrupt institutions. 

That’s what leaders are struggling with today. The old institutional channels are clogged. Messages must compete with myriad sources of information, ranging from good-faith institutions to nefarious players, rumor, and innuendo, before they can reach each of those small groups.

All change is local, now more than ever

In the 1950s, sociologist William Whyte made an observation about the spread of air conditioners. Back then, units were installed in windows, and he observed that these didn’t propagate evenly, but in clusters from building to building. People weren’t buying air conditioners after just hearing about them. They bought one after visiting a neighbor who had one running on a hot day.

Yet this was also the beginning of the era of mass communication. New electronic media, like television and later the internet, were measurable in ways that traditional channels were not. Increasingly sophisticated marketers divided audiences, tracked metrics, and built complicated mathematical models in an effort to make persuasion more predictable and accountable.

Yet the human brain never changed. We evolved to manage relatively small social networks that max out at about 150 people, a phenomenon known as Dunbar’s number. Beyond that, our capacity for trust diminishes greatly. Decades of research have shown that we rarely interact with information directly, but rather filter it through our peer networks. 

What recent events in Serbia, Hungary, and other places have shown is that this process can be countered, but as General Stanley McChrystal has put it, “It takes a network to defeat a network.” While it is true that institutions, nation-states, and oligarchs today have the capacity to broadcast messages that permeate every facet of our lives, it is becoming increasingly clear that people are no longer listening. 

It’s no longer enough to communicate a vision to shape opinions. Leaders today must learn to shape networks.

They’ve won just once in the Premier League, against Ipswich Town, and have gained only eight points from the first 10 matches.

United’s recent ramshackle play has left them in the Premier League relegation zone and provided no definitive answer on Carrick’s capability, leaving the club’s decision makers with a big choice.

Manchester United have opened talks with USMNT boss Mauricio Pochettino as they explore their options to replace Michael Carrick.

Pochettino and the club are reportedly ‘close’ to agreeing a deal after talks on Tuesday between United and representatives for the Argentine.

No decision over his future has yet been made but signs are pointing to the current boss facing the sack as early as today.

CoreWeave stock pops 11% after outlook raise eases AI slowdown concerns

📅 2026-11-11  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

CoreWeave shares jumped 11% in premarket trading Wednesday after the neocloud posted quarterly earnings that beat estimates and raised its guidance for the year.

Neoclouds such as CoreWeave, Nebius and IREN have come under scrutiny this month as investors assessed the sustainability of the AI trade. CoreWeave, in particular, sparked alarm when spreads on its CDS widened past 11%, generally viewed as a distress level, as investors have to pay up for protection against default.

Revenue jumped 168% to $3.7 billion in the third quarter, the company said in a Tuesday statement.

The three months to Sept. 30 marked a "breakout quarter" for CoreWeave, CEO Michael Intrator said, adding that growth was supported by multiple sources of funding, including financing backed by customer contracts and around $9 billion in customer prepayments and deposits.

Shares were 10.8% higher as of 5:08 a.m. ET. Other neclouds were also higher in premarket trading, with Nebius up 11.4% and Iren up 7.9%.

CoreWeave expects $1.275 billion to $1.375 billion in adjusted operating income on $13 billion to $13.3 billion in revenue. Analysts surveyed by Visible Alpha were looking for $1.07 billion in adjusted operating income, and analysts polled by LSEG had expected $12.86 billion in 2026 revenue.

The neocloud has done well to allay concerns about financing by moving away from unsecured bond financing, Zacks Chief Equity Strategist John Blank told CNBC's "Squawk Box Europe."

"It might be the bottom for CoreWeave," Blank said Wednesday. "But the shares are so beaten up, you have to believe at this point there's more value on the upside than the down."

Tune in at 9:50 a.m. ET as CoreWeave CO Michael Intrator joins CNBC TV to discuss the company's earnings. Watch in real time on CNBC+ or the CNBC Pro stream.

— CNBC's Jordan Novet contributed to this report.

Q: “Are My Child’s Tummy Aches a Sign of Stress?”

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Here are some patterns to observe to discern whether your child’s stomach pain is associated with anxiety or an underlying medical condition.

Q: “My child has anxiety and often complains of tummy aches. How do I know if the stomachaches are related to anxiety or a medical condition? How do I know when to see a doctor?”

As parents, it’s tricky to untangle common complaints about stomachaches from chronic anxiety, an underlying medical problem, or even school avoidance. Here are a few ways to tell the difference, and clear signs that it’s time to seek medical help.

Understand the mind-body connection. Our gut and brain are in constant conversation. When a child feels overwhelmed, scared, or worried, their body will respond with stomach pain, nausea, constipation or diarrhea, and a generalized discomfort. These symptoms are not imagined or exaggerated; they are physical reactions to emotional stress.

[Download: 5 Ways to Improve Emotional Control at Home]

Observe timing and patterns. When are stomachaches happening? Stomach pain related to anxiety trends to show up during specific times: before and around school, tests, social events, and bedtime. Does your child’s stomachache dissipate after these types of events, or when they are comforted or distracted?

Are your child’s stomach pains taking place at night? Are they making it difficult for your child to eat? Are they losing weight? Is your child experiencing chronic diarrhea or do they have blood in their stool? Are they vomiting? These symptoms are more indicative of an underlying medical condition, especially if they become worse over time.

Keep a log. Track when your child has stomach pains to help you discern whether it’s due to anxiety or an underlying medical condition. Occasional stomachaches that happen during stressful times are common.

However, if your child complains of a stomachache several times each week for more than a month, consult with your pediatrician or seek a gastroenterologist.

[Read: “5 Ways to Reframe Anxiety for Your Worried Teen”]

And remember: You don’t have to choose between taking anxiety seriously and taking physical symptoms seriously. Both deserve attention. By watching for patterns, listening carefully, and seeking help when needed, you’re doing exactly what your child needs: letting them know that any pain they experience matters.

Liz Nissim, Ph.D., is a clinical and school psychologist specializing in parenting and executive function coaching for neurodivergent children and teens.
